## Snapshot
Two-segment infrastructure-tech compounder. **Semiconductor Solutions** (~68% of rev in Q2 FY26) is now dominated by **AI semis** — custom XPUs/ASICs co-designed for hyperscalers/frontier labs plus the merchant AI-networking franchise (Tomahawk switches, Jericho fabric, 200/400G SerDes, 1.6T DSPs, CPO/optics). **Infrastructure Software** (~32%, GM ~93%) is VMware/VCF + legacy CA/Symantec, a sticky ARR annuity. The investment case is almost entirely AI-ASIC: AVGO is the de-facto primary silicon partner to **Google TPU** (12-yr relationship, 14 ASICs to date), plus Anthropic, OpenAI, Meta MTIA, and two unnamed customers (JPM believes ByteDance + Alibaba; per Harlan Sur, 2026-06-05). It also designs custom XPUs at TSMC and depends on outsourced foundry/HBM/substrate supply (FY25 10-K). Bench-marked as a structural AI-networking winner: UBS pegs AVGO at ~29% of the merchant AI-networking TAM by 2028 (UBS 2026 overview, 2025-12-22).

## At a glance — product · buyer · supplier
| | |
|---|---|
| **Sells (top 3)** | 1) Custom AI XPUs/ASICs (e.g. Google TPU) · 2) AI/DC networking silicon (Tomahawk, Jericho, SerDes/DSPs/optics) · 3) Infrastructure software (VMware/VCF + CA/Symantec) |
| **Main buyer(s)** | Hyperscalers / frontier labs — Google, Anthropic, OpenAI, Meta + 2 unnamed; top-five end customers a significant share of net revenue (FY25 10-K) |
| **Key suppliers** | TSMC foundry (CoWoS); HBM (SK Hynix/Micron/Samsung); substrates (Singapore/SEMCO glass); back-end packaging (ASE FoCoS) |

It is not a Broadcom statement and carries no AVGO rating or estimate. The only hard fact is the October-2027 cut-off date.** ➤ **The argument is nonetheless worth holding because it is falsifiable and it is a genuine tension in the VMware strategy: strict enforcement forces a hardware refresh, and a hardware refresh is the single moment at which an incumbent hypervisor is easiest to displace. Broadcom's own economics may therefore argue for the flexibility Nutanix expects. Watch for enforcement signals as October 2027 approaches.**

- 🔴🔴 **BROADCOM'S ROLE, WHICH IS THE PART THAT MATTERS FOR THIS PAGE: *"The new article introduces that **AVGO is to provide RESIDUAL VALUE SUPPORT, covering any shortfall if lease payments lapse and resale doesn't fully repay A1 and A2 holders ($31bn OF $36bn)**. We do not expect said RV support to be **treated as debt** by the company, agencies, or investors, consistent with the press's comment that **AVGO's ratings and balance sheet should remain unaffected.**"*** ➜ **$31bn of $36bn is ~86% of the stack sitting behind Broadcom's residual-value support. Read that against the figure this wiki already carries from 08-24 — that Broadcom's June deal *"carried a guarantee covering ~82% of the deal"* — and the two are the same order and almost certainly the same programme seen three months apart. ⚠️ They are NOT confirmed to be the same transaction and should not be double-counted.**
- 🔴 **THE ACCOUNTING PRECEDENT MS USES, AND IT IS THE MOST TRANSFERABLE PART OF THE NOTE: *"This mechanism builds on the incorporation of a **residual value guarantee in the [[META]]/Beignet deal**. That set-up differs as META not only supports the asset (data center shell) but also LEASES it, but it is instructive for assessing AVGO risk. **For the RVG, NO LIABILITY IS RECORDED IN META'S 10-Q as the payments are "NOT PROBABLE," while NO DEBT ADJUSTMENT IS CURRENTLY MADE BY S&P given headroom between the RVG and a THIRD-PARTY APPRAISAL VALUE** (the latter we assume is aided in this case of chips by a **compute supply-demand imbalance**)."*** ⚠️⚠️ **That final parenthesis is the whole risk in one clause: the off-balance-sheet treatment holds because a third-party appraisal says the collateral is worth more than the guarantee — and the appraisal is supported by the current compute shortage. It is a mark-to-shortage accounting treatment. If the compute supply-demand imbalance normalises, the appraisal moves, the headroom narrows, and the debt adjustment question re-opens. Cross-file to [themes/hyperscaler-capex](themes/hyperscaler-capex.md), where the Beignet/RPLDCI structure is already priced.**

- 🔴 **THOR ULTRA, AND THE CONVERGENCE ARGUMENT: *"Broadcom provided details on its **Thor Ultra** chip… Thor Ultra combines **800GbE with MULTI-PATH PACKET SPRAYING, OUT-OF-ORDER PLACEMENT, SELECTIVE RETRANSMISSION and RECEIVER-DRIVEN CONGESTION CONTROL**, allowing the NIC to intelligently distribute traffic across multiple paths while maintaining near-line-rate utilization. **This increasingly resembles [[NVDA]]'s Spectrum-X philosophy, where rather than relying on conventional RoCE, both vendors are pushing more congestion management, routing intelligence and resiliency into a tightly coordinated NIC-PLUS-SWITCH SYSTEM.**"*** ➜ **The strategic read is that the merchant-Ethernet story is no longer "cheap standard parts versus proprietary fabric" — it is two vertically-coordinated NIC+switch systems competing on the same axis. That REMOVES one of the standard bull arguments for merchant networking (openness/commodity pricing) and replaces it with a straight capability contest.** ⚠️ **NVIDIA's counter, from the same conference: *"Spectrum-X now stripes traffic across MULTIPLE INDEPENDENT ETHERNET PLANES (splitting a single NIC's ports across different switches) and uses endpoint intelligence to dynamically steer around congestion or failures — effectively attacking the same utilization and fault-isolation problems Broadcom is addressing with Thor Ultra."* William Blair reads the tighter switch/system integration as a positive for [[ANET]] as a downstream supplier.**
- 🔴🔴 **THE CONTENT NUMBER, AND IT IS THE MOST USEFUL LINE IN THE NOTE FOR THIS PAGE: **each [[AMD]] Helios rack uses 12 TOMAHAWK 6 102.4 Tbps SWITCHES for its scale-up fabric.** *"While AMD touts its own co-design prowess… its networking capabilities notably RELY ON THE BROADER ETHERNET ECOSYSTEM. For example, each Helios rack leverages 12 Tomahawk 6 102.4 Tbps switches for the scale-up fabric (UALoE)."*** ➜ **Broadcom silicon is inside the scale-up fabric of the leading non-NVIDIA GPU rack — 72 MI455 GPUs, single-hop all-to-all UALink-over-Ethernet, 30+ TB of HBM4 exposed. That is merchant switching attaching to a competitor's accelerator programme, and it is the cleanest illustration on this page of why Broadcom's networking franchise is not a bet on any single XPU winning.** ⚠️ **No ASP, no annual rack volume and no revenue figure is given, so this is a content-per-rack datapoint only — do not derive a dollar TAM from it here.**
- ⚠️ **CONTEXT FROM THE SAME NOTE THAT MATTERS FOR THE CUSTOM-ASIC THESIS: [[OPENAI]]'s Jalapeño went *"from initial design to tapeout IN JUST NINE MONTHS"* with an Ethernet-based scale-up domain of **up to 2,048 ASICs**, and *"OpenAI also noted that its **SECOND-GENERATION ASIC IS APPROACHING TAPEOUT, WITH A THIRD GENERATION IN PLANNING**."* William Blair's conclusion: *"custom ASICs can increasingly compete with NVIDIA not by matching GPU generality, but through TIGHT MODEL-SOFTWARE-HARDWARE CO-DESIGN ACROSS MULTIPLE GENERATIONS."*** ➜ **Cuts both ways for this page and both directions should be held: a multi-generation custom-ASIC roadmap at a frontier lab validates the XPU thesis Broadcom's AI business rests on, and it is also a demonstration that a lab can execute silicon without Broadcom being the named partner — the Jalapeño design partner is not identified in this note.** [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/AI_Infrastructure_Competition_Heats_Up_Takeaways_From_Hot_Chips_2026.html)


**🆕🔴🔴 2026-08-25 — JALAPEÑO IS THE BEST ADVERTISEMENT BROADCOM HAS EVER HAD, AND THE CREDIT MARKET SPENT THE SAME DAY REPRICING THE COMPANY.** [[OPENAI]] unveiled **Jalapeño**, its first custom inference ASIC — **built with Broadcom**, announced at Hot Chips 2026 — and SemiAnalysis was given lab access to benchmark it.
- **The verdict:** *"In general first-generation chips are not competitive, but **OpenAI bucks the trend by being industry leading and beating every Nvidia, AMD and Google chip we have been able to test on multiple top open-source models**."* Headline metric: **token throughput per all-in utility MW — "Jalapeño smokes every other chip"**, achieved **without Multi-Token Prediction while the comparison chips use their best MTP configs**. *"Jalapeño's STP output token throughput per MW **surpasses Vera Rubin's MTP results that NVIDIA and CoreWeave published in July**."* On perf/TCO, **Vera Rubin and Jalapeño are head-to-head** — but Jalapeño's figure is without speculative decoding, so *"when speculative decoding is implemented… Jalapeño will serve much lower cost tokens."* (SemiAnalysis · Bryan Shan, Myron Xie, Jordan Nanos, Wega Chu, Clara Ee, Dylan Patel, 2026-08-25)
- **The desk read is explicit about who wins:** *"They called out **AVGO as the clear winner, with Jalapeño validating its networking, physical-design, packaging and supply-chain capabilities and potentially creating a meaningful new ASIC revenue stream**"* (TMTB EOD Wrap, 2026-08-25). SemiAnalysis's own framing on TCO: *"part of the advantage comes from **trading Nvidia's high margins for Broadcom's lower (though still high) margins**. But this is not all of it — [[META]] and [[MSFT]]'s AI ASIC programs not getting off the ground despite being at it far longer shows cost is only one part of the equation."*
- **Broadcom silicon inside the rack, named:** the scale-up fabric uses **six 102.4T Tomahawk 6 switch ASICs per rack for the local 128-XPU domain**, plus **two global-domain switch trays that SemiAnalysis believes carry 2× Tomahawk 6 each (204.8T per tray)**, connecting up to **2,048 XPUs across 16 racks**. **[[AMD]]'s Helios rack, presented at the same conference, ALSO routes its memory-semantic scale-up traffic through Tomahawk 6 switches.** ⚠️ **Two of the three most-watched non-NVDA rack architectures now name Tomahawk 6 as the scale-up switch.**
- **Timing, which caps the revenue read:** *"OpenAI has engineering samples of Jalapeño; **production is currently scheduled to gradually ramp over 2027 with most of the output currently scheduled for Q4 of next year**."* ⚠️ **No unit volumes or ASPs were published in the public article (SemiAnalysis points to its paid Accelerator Model).**

**🆕🔴 2026-08-24 (PM) — THE JUNE TPU FINANCING IS NOW FULLY DECOMPOSED, AND THE NUMBER THAT MATTERS IS THE *COVERAGE RATIO*: BROADCOM GUARANTEED ~82% OF DEAL ONE. NVDA'S COMPETING PLATFORM OFFERS 25%.** (The Information · **Dakin Campbell**, "Filling In the Gaps in Nvidia's $500 Billion Financing Pitch", Special Report, 2026-08-24 — *trade press; the AVGO structure is described as the market's reference precedent*)
- **Deal one, restated with mechanics:** *"a **$35 billion financing deal announced in June** to finance **Broadcom-designed Google tensor processing units** … **Apollo teamed up with Blackstone to raise debt, BUY the TPUs and LEASE them to [[ANTHROPIC]]**, helping the private, unrated company secure far more chips than it likely could have on its own. **Broadcom agreed to backstop $29 billion of the overall debt through a RESIDUAL VALUE GUARANTEE**, making up the difference if the chips were worth less than lenders expected. **The riskiest portion of debt did NOT have a Broadcom backstop.**"*
- 🔴 **"Broadcom's support was key to making that first deal happen, with its guarantee covering roughly 82% of the overall deal."** Against that, **[[NVDA]]'s $500bn platform offers "residual value support of up to 25%."** ➜ **On coverage, AVGO's credit support is ~3.3x NVDA's. Two readings, both live: (a) AVGO bought the socket with its balance sheet and NVDA does not have to; (b) AVGO's structure is the one that actually closed and NVDA's has only MOUs. As of 2026-08-24 the second is factually true — The Information reports the six NVDA firms "have signed memorandums of understanding but haven't executed anything."**
- 🔴 **AND THE COVERAGE RATIO IS ALREADY FALLING ON DEAL TWO — WHICH RECONCILES, WITH A BASIS CAVEAT, TO THE ~$100bn STRUCTURE THIS PAGE LOGGED THIS MORNING.** *"that number may be shrinking for future deals, putting Broadcom's credit support closer to what Nvidia is envisioning. **Bloomberg recently reported that a second Broadcom financing under discussion for MORE THAN $60 BILLION could include a $30 BILLION JUNIOR TRANCHE** — the riskiest debt that's first to absorb losses."*
  - ⚠️ **BASIS DISCREPANCY, FLAGGED NOT RESOLVED.** This page's 08-24 AM entry (UBS) reads the second structure as **~$100bn total = $60-70bn senior secured partially guaranteed + ~$30bn junior**. The Information/Bloomberg reads it as **">$60bn total, INCLUDING a $30bn junior tranche."** **Those cannot both be right: either $60bn is the senior leg (UBS) or it is the whole deal (Bloomberg).** The two differ by ~$40bn of size and by ~25pts of junior share (30% vs ~50%). **Log the basis with every quote; do not average them.**
- **Why the structure exists at all, in the lender's words:** *"So far, when GPU purchases have been financed… there's usually a contract or other backstop from an investment-grade customer like Microsoft or Meta Platforms. **Smaller AI companies without close ties to such benefactors largely haven't been able to access the same financing.**"* ➜ **The residual-value guarantee is the substitute for an investment-grade offtaker. AVGO's guarantee is what makes an unrated Anthropic bankable — which is the same reason it is a credit exposure, not a marketing expense.**
- 🔴 **A CROSS-READ WORTH HOLDING: MS's [[GOOG]] note the same evening prices ~1mn TPUs (~1.3 GW) at ~$35bn to derive $27bn/GW. If that is this transaction, the ~1.3GW of TPUs was ~100% debt-funded at the asset level with AVGO carrying 82% of the residual risk — i.e. the ASP the Street is now marking TPUs to was struck against Broadcom's balance sheet.** ⚠️ Same-transaction inference, not confirmed by either source.

**🆕🔴 2026-08-18 (PRIMARY NOTE, NET-NEW HOUSE ON THIS PAGE) — WELLS FARGO PUTS THE STREET-HIGH FY27 AI NUMBER ON PAPER TWO WEEKS BEFORE THE PRINT: $141.5BN vs STREET $118.8BN, DERIVED FROM GW-DEPLOYED × $/GW RATHER THAN UNITS × ASP. THE NOTE ALSO CARRIES TWO BALANCE-SHEET DISCLOSURES THIS PAGE DID NOT HAVE — RPO AT $164.6BN AND PURCHASE COMMITMENTS AT $128.1BN.**
- **The call:** **Overweight, PT $545** (px $392.43 on 08/17, +38.9% implied), maintained into F3Q26 (Wells Fargo · Aaron Rakers, "F3Q26 Preview: AI Semi Upside & Custom XPU Competition Remain Key Focus; Maintain Above-Consensus FY27-FY28 Estimates", 2026-08-18). **FY27 AI semi revenue $141.5bn vs Street $118.8bn (+19%); FY28 $205.3bn vs $168.2bn (+22%). FY27 EPS $21.70 vs Street $19.45 (+11.5%); FY28 $29.00 vs $26.18 (+10.8%).** F3Q26 itself is modelled essentially in line — **rev $29.449bn vs Street $29.479bn, AI semis $16.037bn vs $16.110bn, EPS $3.22 vs $3.24.** ➤ **Place this against the JPM buyside bogeys already on this page (2026-08-11): Bloomberg FY27 AI consensus $120bn and EPS $19.28. Wells is ~18% above the Street's AI line and is the highest FY27 AI number this page carries. The near-term quarter is NOT where they differ — the entire divergence is FY27/FY28.**
- 🔴 **THE MECHANISM, AND IT IS THE PART TO ARGUE WITH: Wells models Custom XPU revenue as GWs deployed × $B/GW, explicitly rejecting units × ASP.** FY27 builds to **7.581 total GW at a blended $13.5bn/GW**; by customer — **Google TPU 7.00GW @ $14.5bn/GW · Anthropic 2.50GW @ $14.5bn · Meta MTIA 1.00GW @ $12.0bn · OpenAI 1.35GW @ $12.2bn · other 0.425GW @ $10.5bn** — running to **$247.5bn total AI semi revenue by FY29**. Management's own stated range is **$10–20bn/GW and expected to stay "relatively stable"**; Wells notes **Epoch AI data implies only ~$11bn/GW, and TPU v7 at ~$11,740 per chip.** ⚠️ **Wells sits at $13.5bn blended — above Epoch's ~$11bn implied and mid-range vs Hock's band. The FY27 gap to Street is therefore substantially a $/GW assumption, not a GW assumption; at Epoch's ~$11bn the same GW ladder gives roughly $110bn, i.e. BELOW consensus. This is the single input to stress.** ⚠️ **These are CONTENT-per-GW figures (AVGO chip revenue ÷ GW deployed) — do not net or chain them against the $34.5bn ÷ ">1GW" SPV debt-per-GW ceiling logged in the 08-17 block below; different objects.**
- 🆕 **NET-NEW #1 — RPO: total RPO $164.6bn exiting F2Q26 with ~30% to be recognised over the next 12 months, vs $45.0bn exiting F1Q26 (~33% NTM) and $24.7bn in the year-ago quarter.** Implied 12-month-forward recognised RPO is **~33% of Wells' NTM revenue estimate**, a materially higher contribution than the prior several quarters. **The F2Q26 10-Q adds language the prior filings did not carry: the commitments include obligations under a long-term contract (SINGULAR — Wells reads this as Google) for custom AI ASICs entered into in F3Q26.** ➤ **A single-contract disclosure appearing in the quarter AFTER the balance-sheet date is a forward tell: it means the F3Q26 print will carry a step in RPO that is already contracted and already disclosed.**
- 🆕 **NET-NEW #2 — SUPPLY-SIDE COMMITMENT: purchase commitments of $128.1bn exiting F2Q26, split $55.2bn FY27 / $72.9bn FY28**, primarily inventory; inventory balance **$4.328bn vs $2.962bn in F1Q26**. Wells frames this as the supply-chain evidence behind the ">$100bn FY27" line, alongside management's stated visibility through FY27 and "work in progress" on FY28/FY29. ⚠️ **THE NOTE'S OWN BASE-PERIOD FIGURES DO NOT SURVIVE A SANITY CHECK AND ARE NOT ADOPTED: it states the $128.1bn is "up from only $54 million exiting F1Q26" and $296m a year ago, against a past peak of $1.441bn in F3Q21 — a ~2,370× sequential step. The F2Q26 LEVEL is the disclosure that matters and is carried; the F1Q26 comparative is flagged as unverified pending the 10-Q, on the same principle applied to the front-page-vs-exhibit checks logged wiki-wide.** The RPO series has the same shape ($45.0bn → $164.6bn q/q) and deserves the same check.
- **Customer roster now stated as SIX custom XPU customers** — Google TPUs (~85%+ of custom XPU revenue in FY25 on Wells' read of Epoch data), Anthropic (buying Google TPUs/racks), Meta MTIA, OpenAI, **and two unnamed** (Wells notes **ByteDance, Fujitsu and Apple have been previously reported** — none confirmed by AVGO). The 5th customer is logged as **$1bn initial order, XPU, late 2026**. ➤ **Consistent with the roster this page already carries; the incremental fact is that Wells' 7.6GW FY27 estimate deliberately excludes any Apple or Anthropic-direct XPU, so both would be additive rather than embedded.**
- **AI networking, quantified: FY26/FY27/FY28 at $19.4bn / $37.0bn / $51.1bn**, with F3Q26 modelled at **$5.559bn ≈ 35% of AI semis** (vs ~40% in F2Q26 and ~33% in F1Q26). **Management's framing is unchanged and is the constraint: 40% is "likely a high watermark," ~30% the reasonable long-term share.** Supporting checks: **Celestica** flags scale-up wins for **AMD Helios** (CYE26 samples, 1H27 ramp), **OpenAI** (samples '26, ramp '27 into '28) and one of its largest customers; **Arista** reiterates 5–7 scale-up rack opportunities with the majority of Ethernet scale-up in 2027–2028.
- **SUPPLY/PACKAGING — a schedule PULL-IN worth logging: Tomahawk 6 is now to be manufactured at Broadcom's new Singapore advanced packaging fab, with production output commencing in AUGUST versus the prior DECEMBER plan.** Separately, the **FOPLP panel-level JV with PowerTech (announced late-July)** is reported (TaipeiTimes, cited by Wells) to qualify in **2H2026 with shipments by mid-2027, Phase 1 ~3,000 panels/month (510×510mm) plus ~3,000 more in 2027, ~95% target yield.** Concentration reminders from the filings: **~95% of AVGO's wafers were produced by [[TSM]] in 1HFY26, and >75% of manufacturing materials come from five suppliers, all single-source.**
- **The distributor cross-check, which is the cleanest independent read on the quarter: AVGO disclosed one distributor at ~42% of F2Q26 semiconductor sales (~38% trailing four quarters) — Wells believes this is WT Micro, and finds R²=0.97 between WT Micro's revenue and AVGO's implied contribution. WT Micro's three months ending July: NT$562.6bn, +139% y/y, +10% q/q (DC & Server ~NT$350bn, +274% y/y); 3Q26 guide NT$572–612bn, +80% y/y and roughly flat q/q, with management citing datacenter deliveries increasing substantially in 4Q26 and accelerating sharply again in 2027.** ➤ **Wells reads this as F3Q26 tracking broadly IN LINE — i.e. the distributor data does not support an F3Q26 blowout, and the acceleration it does support is 4Q26/2027. That is the same shape as the guide-not-the-quarter debate already on this page.**
- **Software:** F3Q26 guided **$8.9bn (+31% y/y)**, management calling it a consistent baseline; Wells models software GM ~93% and non-GAAP op margin **79–81%**, with higher core-count CPUs driving accelerating VMware growth. [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/81b52470-2170-4d31-a7b2-2d8f34f632ce.html)

**🆕🔴 2026-08-17 — AN INDEPENDENT RESEARCH SHOP RE-DERIVES THE AI XPV STRUCTURE FROM THE DISCLOSURES AND IT LANDS ON THIS PAGE'S NUMBERS TO THE DOLLAR — WHICH MAKES THE $29BN MAX EXPOSURE A CORROBORATED FACT RATHER THAN A JPM MODEL. IT ALSO ADDS THE THREE THINGS THE PAGE DID NOT HAVE: THE DRAW SCHEDULE, THE DEFAULT WATERFALL, AND A CEILING ON WHAT THE BACKSTOP IS WORTH.**
- 🔴 **THE STRUCTURE, RESTATED FROM AN INDEPENDENT SOURCE: "investors committed $34.5bn of debt to finance more than 1 GW of Google TPU systems for Anthropic, led by Apollo-managed funds with Blackstone and global banks. Of that, $30bn benefits from BROADCOM's support and $4.5bn does not."** Tranching and pricing: **A1 $6bn at Treasuries +1pp; A2 $24bn at 5.75%; B $4.5bn at 8.5%** — **A1/A2 senior AND Broadcom-backstopped, B junior and NOT backstopped**; **Broadcom's reported MAXIMUM EXPOSURE is $29bn** (Epoch AI · Campbell Hutcheson, "Will financing bottleneck AI compute? An Anthropic case study", 2026-08-12). ✅ **RECONCILES EXACTLY WITH WHAT THIS PAGE ALREADY CARRIED FROM JPM (2026-06-16) AND AVGO'S OWN 10-Q: $6bn + $24bn + $4.5bn = $34.5bn of DEBT, and adding the $800m Apollo/Atlas SP equity gives the "$35bn first tranche" figure on the page. Every rate matches (T+100bp / 5.75% / 8.5%), as does the $29bn cap.** ➤ **This is the first time the structure has been reconstructed from disclosure by a party with no banking relationship — it moves the $29bn from "JPM's read of the 10-Q" to a corroborated number, and it removes the residual worry that the page was carrying a single house's arithmetic.**
- 🆕 **NET-NEW #1 — THE DRAW SCHEDULE, WHICH THE PAGE HAD NO VERSION OF: "funding is drawn as systems arrive — roughly 16 stages over a little more than a year, with around $24bn expected to be paid out by summer 2027."** Broadcom's exposure therefore **grows as capital is drawn and then declines as Anthropic pays**. ➤ **This gives the $29bn cap a TIME SHAPE for the first time: the peak is not now, it builds through ~mid-2027. Any exposure-to-equity or exposure-to-FCF ratio computed off today's drawn balance understates the peak, and the 10-Q disclosure should be re-read each quarter for the drawn figure rather than the cap.**
- 🆕 **NET-NEW #2 — THE DEFAULT WATERFALL, STATED: on an Anthropic default Broadcom "can take over the lease or arrange a rack sale, then covers remaining A1/A2 shortfalls."** ➤ **So the guarantee is second-loss against the RACKS, not first-loss against the lease — which makes the recovery assumption on used TPU racks the single most important unmodelled input in the whole structure, and it is precisely the assumption Bernstein's "residual-value risk" flag (08-11, below) was pointing at.** Epoch is also explicit on the cash question: **Broadcom does NOT supply the $30bn upfront** — it lends its credit, not its balance sheet.
- ⚠️ **NET-NEW #3 — A CEILING ON WHAT THE BACKSTOP IS WORTH, WITH THE HEDGE THE SOURCE ITSELF ATTACHES: the A2-vs-B spread of 2.75pp (5.75% vs 8.5%) is an UPPER BOUND on the value of Broadcom's backstop to lenders — "it also reflects seniority," so it is not a precise estimate.** ➤ **Useful as a bound, dangerous as a price. Do NOT multiply 2.75pp × $30bn and call it the annual value of the guarantee — the spread embeds the A1/A2-vs-B seniority difference as well as the credit support, so the true backstop value is somewhere BELOW that product.**
- ⚠️ **THE ONE PLACE EPOCH'S FRAMING DOES NOT MAP CLEANLY ONTO THIS PAGE, AND IT MUST NOT BE CHAINED: Epoch ties the $34.5bn to "more than 1 GW" of TPU systems, while this page carries AVGO CONTENT at $10–20bn/GW (Hock's own range) and Jefferies' $11bn/GW. Those are different objects — the SPV's debt buys complete rack systems at Google's grossed-up resale price, not AVGO's chip content — and ">1 GW" is an open-ended LOWER bound, so $34.5bn ÷ 1 GW is a CEILING on debt-per-GW, not a $/GW mark. Never net it against, or chain it into, any content-per-GW figure on this page.**
- **Context Epoch supplies for the demand-durability side of the debate: in November 2025, with LESS THAN $9bn of annualized revenue, Anthropic announced plans to invest $50bn in American compute infrastructure — and nearly $50bn of debt tied to that buildout was assembled in EARLY 2026, BEFORE Anthropic's revenue spiked.** Epoch's headline conclusion: **financing is unlikely to be the immediate limit on frontier compute growth, PROVIDED suppliers that benefit from the deployment absorb part of the risk.** ➤ **Read as demand-durability positive and risk-transfer negative in the same breath: vendor-supported financing is what makes these deployments fundable, and AVGO is the vendor doing the supporting.** See [themes/custom-asic-tpu](themes/custom-asic-tpu.md) for the full structure and the parallel [[GOOG]]-supported datacenter leg.

**🆕 2026-08-16 (PRIMARY NOTE) — MIZUHO REITERATES AVGO AS THE ASIC LEADER WITH A FOUR-PROGRAM 2027E+ RAMP, AND MAKES AVGO ONE OF THE TWO NAMED DRIVERS OF A >75% Y/Y COWOS YEAR.**
- **"AVGO should remain the ASIC leader with TPU/MTIA/Jalepeno/Baltra all potentially ramping in 2027E+"** — four named programs, which is the fullest program list on this page from Mizuho (**Jalepeno** is the [[OPENAI]] inference part already logged here as "Jalapeño"; **Baltra** is net-new to this page as a Mizuho-named program). **CoWoS growing >75% y/y in 2027E with [[NVDA]] and AVGO the key customers.** **Mizuho reiterates Outperform, PT $530** (px $392.99) — **a reiteration of the 07-11/08-09 mark, not a new one** (Mizuho · Vijay Rakesh, "AI Server Call: 2027 VR with HBM and NPO/CPO, Adv. Packaging EMIB-T/CoPoS Ramps", 2026-08-16). AVGO is also in the note's preferred 2027E upside list (**[[DELL]] / [[CRDO]] / [[NVDA]] / AVGO / [[LITE]]**).

**🆕 2026-08-15 — TWO READ-THROUGHS INTO THE PRINT, ONE STRUCTURAL AND ONE ON POSITIONING.**
- 🔴 **STRUCTURAL — the Alchip 2Q26 analyst meeting, which is the clearest outside view of merchant-ASIC economics this week** (MS · Charlie Chan, 2026-08-15; Alchip has no wiki page, filed as read-through — full detail on [MRVL.md](MRVL.md)). **The margin mechanic is the part AVGO holders should carry: Alchip's gross margin fell 50.2% → 35% q/q on higher TURNKEY mix and is guided DOWN AGAIN to 20-30% in 3Q26, full-year low-to-mid 20s%, on a 10-20% NRE / 80-90% turnkey revenue split.** ➜ **Volume ramps compress ASIC gross margin because turnkey silicon displaces high-margin NRE. AVGO's scale and IP content make its mix far richer than a pure design-service house's, so the levels do not transfer — but the DIRECTION does, and it is the mechanism behind the "AI revenue is dilutive to corporate GM" pattern UBS's desk names across the hardware complex this week.** Also net-new: **"all CSPs will have an internal front-end design team"** and **"WINNERS WIN ALL as CSPs won't reach out [to] the company [that] has no track record"** — an incumbency argument that favours AVGO, stated by a competitor. ⚠️ **Alchip's "customers are increasing ASIC usage — 3-4x cost saving compared to current GPU" is unbenchmarked and non-TCO. NOT ADOPTED.**
- **POSITIONING — and it is the most cautionary read on this page into the print:** *"**AVGO longer term fundamentals remain attractive, but many believe DATA CENTER EXPECTATIONS HAVE BECOME STRETCHED, potentially LIMITING NTM UPSIDE INTO THE PRINT**"* (UBS Sector Sales · Robert Ruple, 2026-08-15 — sales commentary, *not* UBS Research). ⚠️ **The same note lists AVGO on BOTH its SHORT-BIASED and its DEBATED lines (and lists it twice on the debated line) — internally inconsistent, flagged wiki-wide this run. Take the prose, not the lists.** ➜ **Read against the BofA Credit downgrade below: the credit desk's objection is the FINANCING STRUCTURE, the equity desk's objection is the EXPECTATIONS BAR. Two different bears on the same name in the same week, neither about the business.**
- **Also in the flow, unadopted:** JPM is running a **buy-side results survey covering AVGO, MRVL, NVDA and SNPS** ahead of the late-cycle prints (JPM · Joshua Meyers, 2026-08-14) — worth watching for the published distribution, same as BofA's NVDA bogey poll.

**🆕🔴 2026-08-11 — BofA CREDIT DOWNGRADED AVGO TO MARKETWEIGHT FROM OVERWEIGHT, AND THE REASON IS THE FINANCING STRUCTURE, NOT THE FUNDAMENTALS. Separately, a second broker put a named technical problem on TPUv9. AVGO -1.5%.**
- **🔴 The credit downgrade:** BofA Credit moved AVGO to **Marketweight from Overweight**, saying **"XPV financing creates more complicated spread and RESIDUAL-VALUE risk"** (TMTB EOD Wrap, 2026-08-11). ➜ **This is the AVGO analogue of what happened to [[NVDA]] the same day (CDS +5bps to 77bps vs CDX IG 52bps): the equity desks are treating vendor/structured financing of AI infrastructure as demand-enabling, and the credit desks are pricing it as a balance-sheet liability. Bernstein explicitly links the two, noting the [[NVDA]] $500bn platform "is similar to the recent AVGO/Blackstone/Apollo deal, which was a catalyst for Broadcom (shares are +7% since the announcement)"** (Bernstein / Tyler Seidman, 2026-08-11). **The same structure produced an equity re-rating and a credit downgrade. Both are now on the page.**
- **🔴⚠️ THE COMPETITIVE ITEM, single-source and flagged as such: Deutsche Bank attributes part of [[MEDIATEK]]'s raised 2027 AI-ASIC share guide (10-15% → 15-20%, ≈$14bn of revenue) to "increased allocation of [[GOOG]]'s TPU volumes… while confidence is likely growing on 2028 partly IN THE FACE OF THERMAL CHALLENGES AT BROADCOM WITH THEIR TPUv9 PART"** (DB / Robert Sanders, "MediaTek — Googly eyes", Buy, TP TWD5,000, 2026-08-11). ⚠️ **Unconfirmed by any other source in today's flow. But it is the first *named technical* reason offered for MediaTek taking TPU share rather than the generic dual-sourcing argument, so it needs verification rather than dismissal.** Corroborating on the share arithmetic (not on the thermal claim): **MS/Charlie Chan calls MediaTek's 15-20% guide "broadly in line with Joe Moore's assumption that MediaTek could secure a 20% revenue share in TPU programs," with MediaTek expecting further gains in 2028 on 2nm TPUs; GUC has also expressed interest in TPU compute-die back-end design services** (MS TMT desk, 2026-08-11).
- **JPM's published F3Q26 bogeys ahead of the 2026-09-02 print (~4:15pm ET, 5pm call) — Joshua Meyers buyside survey, 2026-08-11: F3Q26 revenue $29.4bn guide / $29.45bn Bberg · F3Q26 AI Semis revenue $16bn guide / $15.9bn Bberg · F3Q26 EBITDA 68% (guide & Bberg) · F4Q26 guide marks $34.73bn rev / $21.3bn AI semis / 68% EBITDA · FY27 AI revenue $120bn and EPS $19.28 Bberg · FY28 AI revenue $229bn and EPS $25.69 Bberg.** 🔴 **The survey's live question is the guide itself: "Does Hock take up the '27 AI guide from $100b (to what?) and/or announce additional AI project wins?"** — i.e. **Bloomberg consensus at $120bn already sits 20% above management's own $100bn FY27 AI guide.**
- **Sector context: JPM/Harlan Sur names AVGO among the best-positioned in both compute and networking on June WSTS data, with the industry forecast raised to $1.68T CY26 / $2.25T CY27** (2026-08-11).

- Rev **$22.2B** (+48% y/y), EPS **$2.44** vs Street $22.1B/$2.39 — beat consensus but below buyside bogeys (~$11.5B AI / ~$18.5B Jul-Q AI). GM 77.1%, op margin record **67.3%**, EBITDA 69%. FCF $10.3B (Q2 FY26 earnings, 2026-06-03).
- **AI semis $10.8B** (+143% y/y); networking ~40% of AI rev — Hock flagged 40% as "the ceiling," normalizing toward ~30%. AI bookings **>$30B** vs $10.8B shipped; visibility "all the way to 2028."
- **Q3 FY26 guide:** total rev **$29.4B** (+84% y/y, above Street $28.6B); **AI semis $16.0B** (Street $16–17B, buyside ~$18.5B — the clear miss); GM down ~310bps q/q to **~74%** on ASIC mix; op margin held at 67%.
- **FY26 AI semis $56B** (~+180% y/y). **FY27 AI reiterated ">$100B"** ("easily exceed") — **no raise** vs Street ~$116B; that omission, not the print, drove the **-12.6%** reaction (briefing 2026-06-06).
- New: **20GW XPU platform** (Apollo/Blackstone SPV) financing compute for Anthropic + OpenAI through 2028; first $35B tranche launching. ~**10GW shipments in FY27**, back-half loaded, with "a lot more gigawatts" in FY28 (Hock, Q2 FY26). Hock: "No racks… all a chip business only" — content/GW rises as CPU cores/HBM embed into XPUs (agentic).
- **AI XPV JV financing structure (new detail):** the Broadcom/Apollo/Blackstone SPV (manufacturing Google TPUs for Anthropic) is funded with **$800M Apollo/Atlas SP equity + $6B A1 Notes (~T+100bp) + $24B A2 Notes (~5.75%) + $4.5B Series B Notes (~8.5%, no guarantee)**. The A1/A2 tranches carry a **AVGO residual-value guarantee** (mid-BBB private IG rating); per AVGO's 10-Q the backstop on the customer's 5-yr lease obligations has a **$29B max exposure** (decreasing as leases amortize). JPM pegs the JV's all-in pre-tax cost of capital in the low-6% area — "a paradigm to bring private IG capital to bear" and "easily ported to public markets," though it does not solve the asset/duration mismatch (JPM "AI Capex 2.0", 2026-06-16).
- **2027 AI-revenue framing:** JPM models AVGO at **$150B+ of AI revenue in 2027** (ASIC/XPU + AI-networking), more than double its ~$60B 2026E, viewing the >$100B disclosed 2027 backlog as "conservative messaging." Context: ASICs ~42% of total DC AI-chip units in 2026 → ~53% in 2027, with ASIC units growing ~3x faster than merchant GPUs (+109% vs +39% y/y); seven TPU generations co-designed with AVGO since 2014 (JPM "AI Capex 2.0", 2026-06-16).
- **20GW XPV cadence + C28 scenario (Jefferies "Revisiting on Pullback", 2026-06-29):** Jefferies splits the Apollo/Blackstone 20GW XPV as **~1GW (2026) / 4.5GW (2027) / 14.5GW (2028)** — Anthropic + OpenAI only, with **Google internal and Meta XPUs incremental on top**; the XPV buys chips + rack components from AVGO and pays Google for the IP (GW figures are rack-based, not chip-based). Jefferies' chip-GW-by-customer estimate: **Google 3.5/8.1/15.7, Meta 0.3/1.0/2.5, OpenAI 0/0.8/1.5 (26/27/28E)**. C28 scenario analysis lands **EPS $30-40** with a $225B ASIC mid-point (20GW × **$11B/GW**, low end of the $10-20B/GW range) → ~10x P/E at ~$365; Jefferies models **C28 total revenue ~$229B, EPS $25.19 (base), GM ~68.9%**. Full-year EPS path $7.28(25A)/**$13.52(26E)/$19.77(27E)/$25.19(28E)**. (Jefferies / Blayne Curtis, 2026-06-29)
- **Jalapeño (OpenAI XPU) specs (Jefferies, 2026-06-29):** From OpenAI's public image, Jefferies reads Jalapeño as **6×12-high HBM3e (216GB), a ~740mm² near-reticle-limit compute die + a smaller I/O die, single compute die → ~700W, on N3 → ~$15k ASP**; ships before year-end, production volumes 2H-C27. Estimated **~50% lower cost and ~40% lower power vs Blackwell** at equivalent performance; **Jalapeño2 = A16 + HBM4** (CoWoS-L). Microsoft is the named DC host and a likely interested party (raises Maia-roadmap questions). (Jefferies / Blayne Curtis, 2026-06-29)
- **Why FY27 wasn't raised — supply/readiness, not demand (UBS Tim Rohde, AVGO callback, 2026-06-04):** "Just because he didn't raise a number doesn't mean the number hasn't gone up" — AVGO told UBS FY27 AI **could be raised to $110-120B** but won't be ratcheted "every quarter" (a style choice, also a "dig at Marvell"); a new CFO transition is part of it. UBS reads investors as already at **~$150B FY27** so the omission "wouldn't have made much difference." Bookings **3x shipments** in the quarter. The real signal: **FY28 steps up materially more than FY27-over-FY26** — the 20GW SPV implies ~15GW for Anthropic+OpenAI parked into FY28 beyond their FY27 GW. GM held to **~74% over the next 3-4 quarters** (down only on faster AI-semi/XPU mix, *not* HBM and *not* racks — "all chips now"); op margin held/possibly up. Constraint is **backend/substrate** (Singapore substrate fab pulled Dec→Aug, tools installing, first product = Google XPUs), not HBM and "not so much supply… more readiness of all the components — powered shell, networking" — so Hock won't ship until the rest is ready.
- **Revenue-per-GW messaging walked down (UBS, 2026-06-04):** AVGO's content-per-GW framing went **>$20 → $20 → "$10-20, varies by customer"** and is positioned as a run-rate number (don't multiply a point estimate by 10 GW); Rohde notes much of NVDA's faster revenue-per-GW comes from networking share, so Hock is more hesitant to claim big per-GW step-ups. **OpenAI compute demand ≈ Anthropic's**, but OpenAI revenue *visibility* is lower; the **OpenAI XPU tapes out late this year, ships 2H FY27, big ramp FY28**. Agentic XPUs carry **substantial embedded Arm CPU cores** (favorable to the software/VMware mix too). The **Anthropic 5GW is all Broadcom (no MediaTek)**.
- **TPU order book & roadmap (JPM Harlan Sur, Semis Spring Series, 2026-05-29):** Broadcom's **TPU order book ≈ 6.5M units for next year** (up from a prior ~5.5-6M check); ships ~$20B AI ASIC+networking in 2025 → **~$60B+ in 2026**, with **>$150B backlog for next year**. Roadmap by customer: in volume on Google **Ironwood/V7 (3nm)** now; won the follow-on **V8i "Sunfish" (3nm)** ramping now/2H + meaningful '27; won next-gen **2nm "Blade Runner"** ramping cal-28. Google is weighing extending Sunfish life by **combining two Sunfish compute chips into one package (~2x inferencing perf, ~2x ASP to AVGO)** vs introducing the 2nm part sooner — **either path doubles+ AVGO's ASP with no shipment air-pocket**; cal-28 TPU demand profile so far is **~+70% growth**. Also ramping **Meta MTIA "Athena"/"Iris"** (step-function up in '26), the **Anthropic rack-scale win (2H, on Google TPU)**, and **OpenAI ASIC + SoftBank Arm XPU in cal-27**. The Google COT/MediaTek "simpler TPU" has had multiple delays and is scrambling to optimize for a Q4 ramp; the 6-yr Google partnership carries guaranteed wins for the next 3 TPU generations through cal-31.
- **Tomahawk 6 sold out (JPM Sur, 2026-05-29):** AVGO's next-gen **102T Tomahawk 6 (3nm)** is shipping 1H and the team is **basically sold out into next year**; AVGO is **qualifying Samsung** to unlock more 3nm supply.

On foundry, UBS sees **Google TPU CoWoS capacity +142% YoY in 2027E**, and pegs Broadcom's TPU v7p Ironwood at **~5kwpm of N3 per 1GW (vs Nvidia's 2-4kwpm)** — giving ASICs a higher TSMC content share of rack value (~10-11% vs 4-6% for GPUs). Overall: increased JPM confidence in AVGO's AI compute + networking runway. (JPM · Harlan Sur, 2026-07-08)

- **Hock Tan @ JPM investor call — primary-source detail (JPMorgan · Harlan Sur "Hock Tan @ JPM", 2026-07-07):** the underlying Hock Tan / CFO Amy Tanner call behind the 07-08 Sur note adds granular color. **Apple:** the extended/expanded **2031** agreement covers ASICs across a broad Apple portfolio incl. **new design sockets over the next five years** and rolls in **"everything including RF"** (the existing FBAR-filter supply deal) — driving a **"massive" Fort Collins fab expansion** (AVGO ~the sole RF FBAR-filter supplier to Apple). **XPU vs GPU:** JPM models custom XPU units crossing GPUs **next year (CY27), ~55% of total XPU+GPU units**; Hock says that for the **~5 frontier-model buyers specifically it's ~50/50 next year**. **Demand concentration:** only **~5 frontier model developers** (Gemini/Google, Anthropic, OpenAI + Meta "catching up" + Grok "debatable") generate **~80% of all compute demand** — neoclouds/hyperscalers "just rent capacity" and are not an independent demand source. **OpenAI first-gen XPU:** co-designed with Broadcom, inference-focused, **first silicon on time, ~9-mo design-to-tape-out**, better perf/watt vs reference GPUs; chip-cadence detail — **~6 months tape-out→prototype, then ~1 year to bring it up in the datacenter**; the follow-on/next-gen chip is "already put in place." **COT/DIY dismissed:** hyperscaler custom efforts are **~2x less performant and ~2x less complex** in chip/packaging/IP; "not a chance" they keep up ("they'll hit a brick wall"); the **Google-COT/MediaTek A8 TPU is "still in the Fab"** (Hock dismisses Taiwan sell-side wafer-count checks as "all incorrect," calling those analysts "the PR agency of MediaTek"). **Tomahawk 6:** fastest switch ramp ever, **sold out for next year, no competitor at TH6** (rivals only at TH5). **400G SerDes:** AVGO the only company with a **working 400G SerDes driving signal on copper >1m**. **XPV/SPV:** the **$35B** structure (Apollo/Blackstone equity + private/insurance debt) buys racks and **leases them to Anthropic on a 5-yr term** — Broadcom **only sells chips** into it (XPUs/Tomahawks/DSPs/NICs), so its **P&L stays chip-margins-only, unchanged**. **Margins/HBM:** will "keep our margins intact one way or the other" vs HBM cost inflation, and **does NOT consign HBM** (HBM is "an integral part of…our XPU" — segregating it means "you're not an ASIC developer"; an implicit jab at ByteDance-style consignment). **Packaging/networking:** multi-die packaging scaling to **up to 8 dies/chip** (CoWoS — SerDes die + multipliers + SRAM + Arm-core orchestration die); expects networking scale-out to standardize on **Ethernet** (vs NVLink/UALink) as clusters grow to ~500-1,000 XPU/GPU. **Google:** the 5-yr partnership retains AVGO's **majority volume share**. (JPMorgan · Harlan Sur "Hock Tan @ JPM", 2026-07-07)

Frames AI DC capex consensus at **$1.2T 2027E (+35% y/y) / >$1.4T 2028E**, ~$50B/GW, cumulative **106GW 2026-30E (~$5.3T, ~4x combined 2025-26E spend)** — AVGO a key beneficiary as GPU/ASIC leader. Mizuho pegs AVGO's 2025 custom-silicon share ~70-80%, potentially growing to **$150-170B by 2028E**, with Tomahawk/Jericho AI-networking outpacing InfiniBand at 800G/1.6T + SUE for scale-up. - **650 Group networking read: OpenAI ASIC (Jalapeño) back-end-loaded to a 4-yr not 3-yr ramp; custom-ASIC proliferation = an Ethernet tailwind for AVGO switching (650 Group · Weckel expert call, 2026-07-14):** in a 650 Group webinar, Alan Weckel reads **OpenAI's custom ASIC (Jalapeño, w/ Broadcom + Celestica) as "very back-end loaded / pushed to the right" — no significant revenue in 2025, a slow 2026 ramp, "more of a 4-year conversation than the 3 they're guiding"** — the gating issue being scaling from proof-of-concept (one data hall) to many data halls, "a challenge for Celestica, Broadcom and OpenAI." Positive offset: **custom ASICs are Ethernet-based "by definition" for scale-out (the only alternative to InfiniBand), and Weckel is confident scale-up also converges on Ethernet** — so as hyperscalers diversify into fleets of accelerators, **Ethernet becomes the unifying fabric across those fleets, a "multiplier effect" on switching vendors** (AVGO Tomahawk). Sizing: the **Ethernet switch market grows to >$200bn well before mid-2030s (from ~$45bn this year)**; total AI networking approaching ~$300bn; **scale-up Ethernet ultimately larger than scale-out by mid-next-decade**. On CPO, AVGO's copper-extension stance is validated — CPO partial 2028, meaningful 2029, crossover next decade; 400G copper certies proven fall-2025.
