(Morgan Stanley · Nowak "Hyperscale capex to $1.4T", 2026-07-14) [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_07_14_brian_nowak_on_hyperscale_capex_raise_to_1_4t_new_.html)
- **MS explicitly defends AVGO's ~80% custom-ASIC share — "displacement premature" (Morgan Stanley · Joseph Moore "In Defense of AVGO's ASIC Share", reiterate Overweight PT $502, 2026-07-14):** MS pushes back directly on the Google→MediaTek share-cede bears — MediaTek TPU participation is **real but displacement fears are premature**, expecting **AVGO to retain ~80% TPU share over time** and calling the "50% share / eventual displacement" scenarios too draconian. Moat = **HBM supply + packaging execution + scale**; frames AVGO a **core AI winner, close #2 to NVDA**, noting AVGO/NVDA have **lagged other AI semis YTD** precisely on this TPU-share overhang (analogy: last year's over-drawn MRVL/Alchip Trainium-share debate). Corroborated on FinTwit by @BenBajarin (07-14): the concern "has never come up… I'd estimate it to remain even higher than 80%," with MediaTek likely doubling its business by end-decade but Broadcom's revenue line still rising. This is the **first explicit sell-side defense** of AVGO's ASIC share, making the debate two-sided vs the GS 50/50 (07-09) / UBS 91%/62% (07-02) / Redburn 6%→30% (06-23) cede marks. Countervailing same-week detail: @jukan05 (07-15) reports **Samsung weighing back-end design of the I/O die for Google's 2nm TPU v10 "Icefish," which Google is co-designing with MediaTek** (TSMC 1.4nm compute + Samsung 2nm I/O; MP ~2028) — a marker that MediaTek's next-gen (v10) co-design role is progressing. (Morgan Stanley / Joseph Moore, 2026-07-14)

## Supply chain & foundry capacity (new)
- **CoWoS allocation raised:** Fubon (dist. Jefferies) lifts AVGO's total TSMC CoWoS allocation **187k→231k wafers** for FY26 (1H26 had been under-utilized on upstream/downstream mismatch); the raise is mainly TPU, with TPU CoWoS lifted **137k→177k** ("Both front-end and back-end…", Fubon/Jefferies). Total TPU output revised to **~3.06M units** vs ~2.57M prior (mix of V6e Trillium, V7p Ironwood, early V8p Sunfish).
- **Anthropic sizing:** per AVGO's announcement Anthropic is a **~$10B customer from CY2Q26**; Fubon models 60–70% recognized in CY26 → **~500–600k TPU V7 (Ironwood) units to Anthropic in CY26**. ASP ~$12k *incl. HBM* (~$2.5k HBM), so **~$10k of AVGO content/unit ex-HBM** (Fubon/Jefferies) — quantifies the HBM pass-through GM headwind the bears flag.
- **Back-end offload:** AVGO's lower-value **networking processors moving to ASE FoCoS** (~10–15% of its CoWoS-equivalent capacity); all AI accelerators (TPU/ASICs) stay on TSMC CoWoS (Fubon/Jefferies). AVGO's AMD-style scale-up packaging "may have a faster ramp-up" than AMD's UALink (Fubon).
- **2027 CoWoS share revised *down* / V9 pushed out:** in its 2027 sector outlook Fubon now cuts AVGO's TSMC CoWoS allocation to **24–25% for 2027** (≈459.6k of ~1.89M wafers; #2 behind NVDA's 50%) on limited volume from its 2nd/3rd ASIC projects (OpenAI/Meta — no samples seen yet, so MP slips to 2H27). **V9 is NOT cancelled** ("remains on track but pushed out") — **V8i MP late 3Q26, V9 MP late 3Q27** → V9i volume very limited in 2027; total AVGO TPU ~6.2M units in 2027 (mix V8i/V9i). AVGO's growth momentum "more meaningful by 2028" (Fubon "Semi 2027 outlook", 2026-06-05).
- **⚠️ SUPERSEDED 2026-07-28 — Fubon cuts the 2027 number again: AVGO's TSMC CoWoS share now 22.3% / 421,800 wafers, down from the 24–25% / ≈459.6k above.** In its updated CoWoS model Fubon's Figure 5 has AVGO at **16.2% (2025) → 22.5% (2026) → 22.3% (2027)** of TSMC CoWoS, or **109,650 → 263,100 → 421,800 wafers**. Two things to hold at once and they point in opposite directions. **(1) The 2026 step-up is enormous and is the part the page under-weights: 16.2% → 22.5% of a pool that itself grows from 675k to 1,170k wafers means AVGO's TSMC CoWoS wafers roughly 2.4x in 2026 — from 109,650 to 263,100 — and then rise another ~60% to 421,800 in 2027.** In absolute terms AVGO is by far the largest gainer after NVDA; the share line flattening at ~22% is a denominator effect, not a volume problem. **(2) The 2027 SHARE cut is nevertheless a genuine downward revision of Fubon's own prior mark, and the wafers it reallocates go to the two names AVGO competes with most directly** — **[[MEDIATEK]] to 7.3% / 137,700 wafers (from Fubon's own 5.3%)** and **[[AMD]] to a 300k total allocation, mostly via ASE** — while **[[NVDA]] holds flat at 50.0%**. So on Fubon's numbers MediaTek's 2027 gain comes partly at Broadcom's expense, which is the exact head-to-head the custom-ASIC share debate on this page turns on. ⚠️ **Basis note: this is TSMC-only CoWoS, a broker capacity-model assumption, not a TSMC or AVGO disclosure — and it says nothing about revenue share, where MS (07-14/07-26) still has AVGO at ~80% of TPU revenue on higher ASP and gross margin even as MediaTek closes the unit gap.** (Fubon "Our advanced packaging, CoWoS forecast adjustments; other updates", 2026-07-28) [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/Our_advanced_packaging_CoWoS_forecast_adjustments_other_up.html)
- **V9/V10 design spec (Fubon):** **V9** = two 3D-stacking chips, four compute dies but only **two on N2 + two on N3** (Google lowering inference cost), moving to **SoIC**; **V10** = four 3D-stacking chips / **8 N2 compute dies**, migrating to **400G SerDes**. AVGO also wins **AWS T4 design service** (front- and back-end; one of two T4 SKUs) — read as share-additive into 2028 via its networking edge (Fubon, 2026-06-05).
- **Full ASIC-by-customer roadmap (Jefferies "Revisiting on Pullback", 2026-06-29):** Jefferies' customer map has AVGO designing Google **TPU v6e Trillium (N4N/HBM3), TPU7x Ironwood (N3/HBM3E/192GB), TPU 8i Sunfish (N3/HBM3E/288GB), TPU 9i Pumafish (N2/3, HBM4/384GB, SoIC), TPU 10i (N2/HBM4/576GB)** — with MediaTek holding the *training* variants (8t Zebrafish, 9t Tigerfish); **Meta MTIA 300/400/500** (MTIA 450 goes to MRVL); **OpenAI Jalapeño (N3/HBM3E/216GB) + V2 (A16/HBM4)**; **Apple Baltra (N3/HBM3E/288GB)**; **SoftBank Izanagi (N3/HBM3E)** — the "two additional customers" AVGO cites are believed to be **Apple** + one other; Jefferies believes **AWS Trainium and xAI are NOT on AVGO's roadmap**. TPU v9 seen **on track (April-27 launch), N2/3 + 400G SerDes (co-packaged copper) + 3D-stacked logic**, with **v10 in late-C27 (~8 logic die, 4-stacked-on-4)** pushing the lead further; MediaTek's Zebrafish only **~60k units in C26** (vs Street 400-450k) deflates the Google-share-loss bear case. (Jefferies / Blayne Curtis, 2026-06-29)
- **Pumafish (TPU 9i) status reversed — cancelled/postponed (JPM Gokul Hariharan, MediaTek note, 2026-07-01):** contra the 2026-06-29 Jefferies roadmap above (which had AVGO's TPU 9i "Pumafish" on-track), JPM's MediaTek note reports Broadcom's competing "Pumafish" ASIC program **reportedly cancelled or postponed**, potentially ceding 2028-29 TPU-v9-generation share to MediaTek. Broadcom is still expected to support **another Google TPU v10 project alongside MediaTek's Icefish**. Separately, JPM (Hariharan, 2026-07-01) flags a possible **fast-inference SRAM-based ASIC in Google's v9 TPU family (codenamed "Merope")** as likely to be awarded to **Marvell rather than Broadcom**, as Google's Compute program expands to match workloads with specific ASICs — a competitive-share nuance for AVGO's custom-silicon franchise. (JPM / Gokul Hariharan, 2026-07-01)
- **Transceiver/networking demand "skyrockets" in 2027:** Fubon checks see AVGO transceiver demand growing "tremendously" in 2027 on hyperscaler (esp. Meta) pull; switch chipsets + transceivers packaged at **ASE** (FoCoS), driving ASE to lift FoCoS capacity to **45kwpm by end-2027** (from prior 30k) (Fubon, 2026-06-05). **Update 2026-07-28 — Fubon raises the ASE FoCoS number again to 50kwpm for 2027, explicitly "to support CPU and networking customers' demand"** (superseding its own 45k above; the 30k→45k→50k path is itself the clearest single measure of how fast the non-accelerator packaging offload is scaling). Positive for the AVGO networking/transceiver leg, which is the part of the book that rides FoCoS rather than CoWoS (Fubon, 2026-07-28).
- **HBM grade:** Samsung HBM3 (NCF, higher-temperature, fails Blackwell qual) "mostly goes to Broadcom, because of their lower standards"; NVDA Samsung adoption <0.5% (StoneX/John Su, 2025-08-20) — second-source HBM optionality for AVGO ASICs.
- **Bit-demand + SerDes/base-die positioning (SemiAnalysis "Scaling the Memory Wall," 2025-08-11):** Broadcom "follows [Nvidia] as TPU and MTIA volumes surge" — named as the **#2 HBM bit-demand driver** via the custom-ASIC programs (Google TPU, Meta MTIA) it designs/ships (p.4). Broadcom (with Marvell) is where hyperscalers go for **high-speed SerDes IP**, necessary for I/O-dense accelerator design (p.20). Also named as a possible **direct "HBM customer"** alongside Nvidia/hyperscalers in the base-die supply-chain discussion (p.41). (SemiAnalysis, 2025-08-11)
- **Asia supply-chain reality-check (Citi/Jefferies "Blaine," Asia recal call, 2026-05-22):** ASIC supply-chain partners still point to **~3M TPU units in 2025** (vs a buy-side that has crept to ~4M; Blaine made the 3M→6M multi-year call 9 mo ago) — sees a gap between buy-side hopes and supply reality despite >2x unit growth. **AI = Broadcom, AT = MediaTek** is settled; the **Broadcom chip is still in development** (given to OSAT in the fall, not yet in production mode), so **2025 is primarily V7 and V8 is largely a 2026 ramp** — possibly a slower V8 ramp than modeled. Broadcom (post-Google event) is "very confident" on the 5-yr deal and **share staying ~80%+**. On the much-cited **Anthropic deal: the original ~$10B of racks (Jul-Q) is now tracking ~$2-3B**, with the follow-on ~$11B also at risk — capacity is being served internally at GCP rather than externally at Fluidstack; **margin higher (chips not racks) but a net dollar cut** — which is why AVGO became a "funding short" with expectations possibly *low* into the print.
- **TPU merchant-sale accounting (B-side recap, hyperscaler callbacks, 2026-04-30):** Google records its external/merchant TPU sales to Anthropic **gross** (Broadcom builds & sells the TPU to Google ~$21k/chip; Google adds a **~10-15% license fee / ~$2-3k per chip** and resells to Anthropic, booking the grossed-up figure in Cloud revenue + backlog) — read-through that AVGO's per-TPU ASP to Google is **~$21k all-in** and that ~$21B of Anthropic TPU orders (the $10B + $11B tranches) ≈ ~1 GW, with a further ~3 GW (~$63B grossed-up) of Anthropic TPU orders for 2027.
- **CoWoS-S exposure (Tomahawk) + TPU-partner read (Jefferies · Sherman Chang "Update with Fubon post-TSMC 2Q earnings", 2026-07-17):** post-TSMC-2Q-earnings call (Jefferies TSMC analyst + Fubon). Broadcom's **networking chips (Tomahawk) use TSMC's CoWoS-S** — a generation where TSMC is capacity-constrained and increasingly leaning on OSATs (ASE, Amkor) — whereas **custom ASICs / GPUs mainly use CoWoS-L**. On the ASIC side, Jefferies' checks put **Google at 12-15M TPUs in 2028 (chip basis)**, with Broadcom one of the two ASIC partners (with MediaTek; exact mix asked but truncated in the source). Jefferies flags very strong AI + server-CPU demand into 2027, benefiting the custom-ASIC and advanced-packaging supply chain. (Jefferies · Sherman Chang "Update with Fubon post-TSMC 2Q earnings", 2026-07-17)
- **BACKFILL / dated Sep-2025 marker — AWS deliberately did NOT pick Broadcom for Trainium, and Google (unlike Amazon) runs its HBM through Broadcom (SemiAnalysis "Amazon's AI Resurgence: AWS & Anthropic's Multi-Gigawatt Trainium Expansion", 2025-09-03).** _Historical/structural context — an ~11-month-old datapoint, NOT a current read; it does not supersede any 2026 figure on this page._ Two markers worth logging. (1) On the **breadth** of AVGO's ASIC franchise: "AWS didn't opt for the world's best custom silicon partner – **Broadcom who has mopped up almost all of the major AI ASIC sockets**. It has chosen **Marvell and Alchip** as design partners who are somewhat weaker technology partners but offer **much more attractive pricing** than Broadcom" — i.e. as of Sep-2025 the only major socket AVGO conceded, it conceded on price, not capability (consistent with the page's standing "MediaTek per-die fee ~50% below Broadcom" and HBM-in-BOM threads). (2) On **content per socket**: Amazon "derives significant savings by **directly procuring their HBM needs**, instead of having it run through their design partners' balance sheet who then stack additional margin on HBM, **which is the case for Google going through Broadcom**" — a dated third-party confirmation that AVGO captures HBM margin at Google but not at AWS, which is exactly the mechanism Redburn (2026-06-23) later identified as the driver of the Google→MediaTek share cede, and which Hock defended on 07-07 ("does NOT consign HBM… HBM is an integral part of our XPU"). (SemiAnalysis, 2025-09-03) [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2025_09_03_ENC_Amazons_AI_Resurgence_AWS_Anthropics_Multi-Gigawatt_Trainium_Expansion_email.html)
- **Design-franchise scale proxy — AVGO's ASIC group is among the largest EDA customers in the world (SemiAnalysis "EDA Market Primer", 2026-05-21):** "**Broadcom's ASIC group alone is estimated to spend $200-500M annually on all-in EDA tool, IP licensing, and emulation hardware**," with **Alchip, GUC and Marvell Custom Silicon adding another $200-400M combined** — turnkey ASIC houses hold the EDA licences on behalf of their hyperscaler clients and run multiple concurrent advanced-node tape-outs. A clean independent proxy for the scale of AVGO's custom-silicon design franchise relative to the merchant-ASIC field (context: total EDA market ~$18B in 2025; systems companies now ~45% of EDA demand). (SemiAnalysis, 2026-05-21) [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_05_21_ENC_EDA_Market_Primer_email.html)

## Networking, scale-up & CPO (new)
- **Scale-up TAM:** MS sizes the scale-up network at **$4B (2024) → $17B by 2029 (34% CAGR)**; NVLink stays the majority near-term, but XPU proliferation opens a greenfield where **AVGO, ALAB, MRVL** all participate (MS Scale-up primer). MS models AVGO **AI-networking revenue >$9B by 2026** (MS Scale-up primer).
- **Scale-up TAM 4x'd to ~$70bn by 2030 — updated MS primer, positive on AVGO (MS "Bringing More Light to Scale-Up Networks — Updated Scale-Up Primer", Meta Marshall / Joseph Moore, 2026-07-13):** MS's updated primer **raises its own scale-up market estimate >4x to ~$70bn by 2030** (from the ~$17bn-by-2029 figure above), on rising capex + cluster sizes. MS is **positive on AVGO ahead of its 2027-28 ASIC ramps and the associated networking pull-through**, framing **scale-up networking as an underappreciated extension of the XPU opportunity** — because AVGO designs the ASIC and owns Ethernet switches, PCIe, SerDes, NICs and optics, it can bundle the surrounding connectivity as custom programs move from silicon into rack-scale deployments. On CPO, AVGO is a **technology leader but the most conservative on adoption**: mgmt argues **200G (eventually 400G) SerDes extend copper within the rack**, supporting MS's view that broad scale-up CPO adoption is more likely **~2028-29** (scale-out customers can adopt earlier for power savings). Broadcom "demonstrated next-gen tech that still extends copper to 6m lengths," challenging NVDA's CPO focus for cross-rack Vera Rubin Ultra connectivity. Copper/optics stay a heterogeneous mix — SUE/ESUN (Ethernet scale-up via Tomahawk) is AVGO's lane. (MS "Updated Scale-Up Primer", 2026-07-13)
- **Scale-up Ethernet (SUE) push:** AVGO strongly advocates SUE as the hyperscalers' preferred scale-up path, citing 5–10x higher switch density and **250ns Ethernet latency**, arguing Ethernet's lack of a specified latency "allows for innovation" (vs AMD's UALink, which specifies latency). MS notes both camps stress ecosystem/interoperability/TCO; SUE/UALink ramp mostly 2027/2028 (MS Scale-up primer; MS Asia on AI Factories).
- **Tomahawk 6** supports both scale-out and scale-up; "multiple large-scale deployments planned with XPUs using Tomahawk 6 for scale-up" (MS Scale-up primer).
- **CPO:** AVGO is a proponent of optics/CPO in the scale-up domain; scale-out CPO from NVDA & AVGO is "garnering more attention" with end customers, and CPO becomes the **main driver of bandwidth increases in scale-up for the latter part of the decade** (CPO Primer — SemiAnalysis). AVGO can also OEM CPO photonic switches into NVDA's Quantum 800 line (StoneX/John Su, 2025-08-20) — a non-competing supplier angle. MS still flags scale-up optics/CPO as a 2028+ event for AVGO (MS Scale-up primer). Redburn corroborates the timing from the equipment side: recent CPO reliability improvements "tip the balance" toward wider adoption into **scale-up networking by 2028/29**, enabled by hybrid-bonding the optical engine (TSMC COUPE/SoIC); it models a cumulative ~50 BESI bonders for CPO by 2030, accelerating from 2H28 (Redburn, 2026-06-15) — a read-through that AVGO's scale-up CPO push is an equipment-gated 2028+ ramp.
- **The underlying SemiAnalysis CPO book, now read in full — AVGO's product ladder, the Meta/Bailly reliability dataset, and the COUPE re-start (SemiAnalysis · Dylan Patel et al, "CPO Book", 2026-01-01).** ⚠️ **_Framework note, Jan-26 vintage — this is the same source cited one bullet above as "CPO Primer — SemiAnalysis," now extracted properly. It PREDATES the entire 2026 flow on this page (MS 07-13 scale-up primer, Redburn 06-15, the 07-29 Lumentum/PhotonCap read-through, Fubon 08-03) and supersedes NOTHING — where a 2026 datapoint contradicts it, the 2026 datapoint stands and this is background._** **(1) Product ladder.** *Humboldt* (TH4) = **25.6T Ethernet, half electrical / half CPO — 12.8T across four 3.2T OEs (32 lanes × 100G)**, SiGe EIC, "served largely as proof of concept." *Bailly* = **51.2T, entirely optical I/O, eight 6.4T OEs (64 lanes × 100G)**, moved to a **7nm CMOS EIC** (which is what allowed 32→64 lanes) and to **FOWLP with ASE/SPIL** as OSAT. *Davisson* = **Tomahawk 6-based, sixteen 6.4T OEs, switch ASIC on [[TSM]] N3, 102.4 Tbit/s per package**, box assembly at **Micas and Celestica**. Broadcom "was among the first companies to offer real CPO-enabled systems and as such is considered a leader in CPO" — but shipped "at very low volumes." ⚠️ **Ecosystem leak worth tracking: NTT Corp is reportedly buying Broadcom's TH6 BARE DIES and building its own CPO systems using proprietary OEs and optical solutions NOT sourced from Broadcom** — expands TH6 CPO demand but decouples the switch socket from AVGO's optics attach. **(2) ⚠️ A density check on the "AVGO leads CPO" framing:** AVGO's 6.4T OE "is very big (**2-3x the width of Nvidia**) and it requires **two FAUs** so its **bandwidth density may be similar to what Nvidia's offering**" — the higher per-engine bandwidth does not convert into a shoreline advantage. **(3) ★ The Meta/Bailly reliability dataset in full** — the ECOC-2025 study that the page's CPO-adoption calls lean on: **OE + laser source in Bailly = 5.4W per 800G delivered vs ~15W for an 800G 2xFR4 pluggable = 65% power saving**; **2.6M device-hour MTBF for CPO vs 0.5-1.0M for 400G 2xFR4 transceivers (550k globally)**; **zero failures and zero uncorrectable codewords through 4M 400G port-device hours**, with only one instance of FEC bin >10 across 1,049k 400G port-device hours over **15 Bailly 51.2T switches**. ⚠️ **SemiAnalysis's own deflation of that dataset, which is the part the bulls drop:** 15M *400G port*-device hours across 15 51.2T switches is only **~7,812 wall-clock hours ≈ 325 days, in a lab**, and the **0.06% unserviceable failure rate carries a blast radius of 64× 800G ports** — "the industry needs far more than just 15 CPO switches tested for 11 months in a lab setting before it pivots towards CPO scale-out switching and commits billions of dollars to this technology." **(4) 🔴 The COUPE transition is a technology RE-START, not an upgrade.** Broadcom's FOWLP approach "doesn't allow scaling beyond **100G per lane** due to excessive parasitic capacitance" (signals must pass through the through-mold vias to reach the EIC), so AVGO "has **committed to COUPE for its future switch AND customer accelerator roadmaps**." But COUPE is biased toward **grating coupling + micro-ring modulators**, whereas every prior AVGO generation used **edge coupling + MZMs** — "both of these choices were simpler from an implementation standpoint, but also less scalable… **Despite Broadcom having the most CPO experience, this change in technical approach means that Broadcom must essentially start fresh on some aspects of their technology.** The question is how much help TSMC can provide." A direct qualifier on the "AVGO is the CPO incumbent" bull leg, and it hands leverage to [[TSM]]. **(5) Where the first volume lands — and the customer problem.** SemiAnalysis expects **AVGO's first mass-produced CPO system to arrive in its CUSTOMERS' AI ASICs, not in switches**, and says AVGO's CPO experience "was a key factor that led **[[OPENAI]] to choose Broadcom**." ⚠️🔴 **But: "Interestingly, [[GOOG]], Broadcom's largest ASIC customer, is the hyperscaler that is MOST HESITANT to deploy CPO in their datacenters. Google's infrastructure philosophy places more emphasis on reliability over absolute performance, and CPO's reliability is a DEAL BREAKER for them. We do not expect Google to adopt CPO any time soon."** That is the sharpest constraint on AVGO's CPO-attach math anywhere on this page — the largest socket is the least likely to take the content — and it is the same argument MS used on 07-23 to rank [[LITE]] #1 in optics. Consistent with, and the customer-side explanation for, MS's 07-13 "technology leader but the most conservative on adoption." **(6) Adjacent marks.** AVGO has been **sampling 224G SerDes since late 2024 in its optical DSPs** and, with [[NVDA]], "lead[s] in high-speed SerDes IP"; it is **deliberately persisting with electrical SerDes over wide I/O (UCIe)** because it must design for copper and expects hybrid co-packaged-copper + CPO "to be here to stay," letting one tape-out serve both — the technical statement of the copper-extension posture the page already carries from MS/Fubon. AVGO is also named among the six vendors able to produce **CW laser units** (with Furukawa, [[LITE]], [[COHR]], Yuanjie, Shijia) — the "platform competitor with its own laser sources" role PhotonCap flagged on 07-29, sourced independently. (SemiAnalysis · Dylan Patel et al, "CPO Book", 2026-01-01) [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/newslettersemianalysiscom_api_v1_post_pdf_postId178153689.html)
- **AVGO is the SOLE scale-up switch supplier in AMD's Helios rack — quantified silicon content per rack (SemiAnalysis "Can AMD break the CUDA Moat? AMD Advancing AI 2026", 2026-07-24):** AMD's 72-GPU Helios rack connects its MI455X GPUs through **12 Broadcom Tomahawk 6 102.4T switch ASICs across 6 switch trays** (2 ASICs/tray), single-tier all-to-all, each GPU carrying 72 lanes of 200G UALoE = 1.8 TB/s uni-di scale-up. AMD has **no scale-up switch of its own**, and SemiAnalysis is explicit on why that makes AVGO sole-source: "**Because Broadcom is the only merchant provider shipping a 100T switch with 200G SerDes, Broadcom is AMD's only choice for the switch**" — framed as a *constraint on AMD* ("AMD cannot reach extreme codesign for the complete rack solution"), which is the mirror image of a locked-in AVGO socket. The inefficiency it creates is also AVGO content: only **432 of each TH6 switch's 512 200G lanes are actively used** (86.4 Tbit/s uni-di per ASIC to the 72 GPUs) because merchant silicon does not divide evenly across 72 GPUs — versus Nvidia's purpose-built **28.8T NVSwitch**, which matches a 72-GPU rack exactly at 400 Gbit/s uni-di per GPU "with no wastage of bandwidth at all." **Second, net-new AVGO content: retimers.** AMD's 200G SerDes cannot hold signal integrity across the copper backplane, so "**up to 85% of its backplane needs to be retimed, requiring over 550 Broadcom ethernet retimers per rack**" (SemiAnalysis models ~85% of scale-up links retimed on Meta's MI455X deployment), placed **in the scale-up switch tray** — extra cost, power and rack-bring-up complexity for AMD, incremental silicon for AVGO (Nvidia's Oberon backplane is completely passive by contrast). **Third, the MI500 generation (2027) scales the switch count ~24x:** it pivots to **co-packaged copper (CPC) + near-packaged optics (NPO)** (9 optical engines at 6.4 Tbit/s uni-di each = 57.6T scale-up) on a **flat one-tier 576-GPU / 8-rack scale-up domain** built from **288 switches on a 115.2T Tomahawk 6 SKU** (vs the 102.4T version). NB the source is internally inconsistent on the MI500 domain size (an earlier passage says 256 GPUs across three racks); the Part-4 architecture section carries the 576-GPU/8-rack/288-switch figures used here. (SemiAnalysis, 2026-07-24) [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_07_24_ENC_Can_AMD_break_the_CUDA_Moat_AMD_Advancing_AI_2026_email.html)
- **Meta's fabric shift moved spend WITHIN Broadcom's portfolio, not away from it — and Grand Teton *increased* Meta's Broadcom reliance (SemiAnalysis "Meta's Infrastructure Team Needs A Culture Reset", 2026-07-21):** two AVGO-relevant reads sit alongside the Rivos/COT negative already logged in Debate/Bear. (1) **Grand Teton (Meta's H100 HGX):** Meta added a switch tray housing **four Broadcom PCIe switches, 16 SSDs and eight NICs** purely to free PCIe lanes for 8 extra SSDs/server. SemiAnalysis's verdict is unflattering to Meta and directly favourable to AVGO's socket: "Ultimately, Meta paid more, had worse TCO, **didn't reduce their reliance on Nvidia networking gear, and increased their reliance on Broadcom**" (the tray has since been cancelled — checkpoint storage was never used as anticipated). Note Nvidia designed the standalone PCIe switch out of the reference design at Hopper by putting PCIe switching into ConnectX-7 — i.e. the AVGO PCIe-switch content here was Meta-specific, not architectural. (2) **DSF → NSF:** Meta's Disaggregated Scheduled Fabric ran on Broadcom **Jericho3-AI (deep buffer) + Ramon3** spine silicon (VOQ + cell-spraying + credit scheduling); as NIC vendors built RoCE congestion handling into silicon, the industry moved to **Non-Scheduled Fabric on shallow-buffer Tomahawk 5**, and **Prometheus and Hyperion now deploy "a vast majority of NSF instances."** For AVGO this is a **mix shift inside its own portfolio (Jericho3-AI/Ramon3 → Tomahawk 5)**, not a lost fabric — while it does open the box to whitebox vendors (Celestica) alongside Arista, and SemiAnalysis models **DSF ~11% more expensive than NSF**. DSF survives where **MTIA** is used (NIC integrated on-chip); GB300 deployments run Nvidia NICs and therefore NSF, so the two co-exist at Meta "for the foreseeable future." (SemiAnalysis, 2026-07-21) [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_07_21_ENC_Metas_Infrastructure_Team_Needs_A_Culture_Reset_email.html)
- **CPO supply-map + scale-up TAM corroboration (07-13/07-14):** a same-day MS CPO note (relayed by @aleabitoreddit, 2026-07-13) places **Broadcom & Nvidia as the switch platforms** in the co-packaged-optics map (lasers $SIVE/$COHR/$LITE; optical engines Lightmatter/Ayar Labs/Marvell-Celestial/POET; SiPho foundry $TSM/$GFS/$TSEM). A former-Intel expert (via @AlphaSenseInc, 2026-07-14) frames **NPO as a temporary fix and CPO the long-term path** (pluggables ceiling ~800G) and names **AVGO and Intel the strongest at building optical engines for CPO/NPO** (NVDA internal-only; META/GOOGL adapt vendor tech) — supportive of AVGO's optical content as CPO matures. Separately @QQ_Timmy (2026-07-13) independently sizes the **AI scale-up network market at >$70bn by 2030 (4x the ~$17bn 2029 est. from a year ago)** with Broadcom a key beneficiary as clusters scale — corroborating MS's same-day 4x scale-up-TAM raise already folded above. (@aleabitoreddit / @AlphaSenseInc / @QQ_Timmy, 2026-07-13–07-14)

## Capstone estimates (house model)
_Source: Capstone official model (`Modelo Avgo pós 2Q26.xlsx`, 2026-06-10)._

| | 2024 | 2025E | 2026E | 2027E | 2028E |
|---|--:|--:|--:|--:|--:|
| Total revenue ($bn) | 54.5 | 68 | 115 | 190 | 315 |
| &nbsp;&nbsp;— AI semis | 14 | 24 | 63 | 132 | 251 |
| EPS — pro forma ($) | 5.36 | 7.18 | 12.89 | 21.07 | 35.96 |

**GW deployed by client (AI):**

| GW | 2026E | 2027E | 2028E |
|---|--:|--:|--:|
| Google | 2.5 | 4.0 | 4.8 |
| Anthropic | 1.4 | 3.2 | 7.0 |
| OpenAI | 0 | 1.3 | 4.3 |
| Meta | 0 | 1.0 | 2.9 |
| Other | 0.2 | 0.4 | 0.6 |
| **Total GW** | **4.1** | **9.9** | **19.6** |
| AI revenue ($bn) | 57 | 139 | 274 |

**House read:** AI semis $24bn → **$251bn** (25-28E, ~80% of revenue in 2028); EPS PF $7.18 → $35.96; custom-ASIC rises from **4.1 → 19.6 GW** deployed, with Anthropic/OpenAI the fastest-growing legs. (Capstone official model, 2026-06-10)

## Debate / thesis
- **Capstone model (house view):** total revenue $68B→$115B→$190B→$315B (2025-28E); AI semis $24B→$63B→$132B→**$251B** (ASIC $18→52→90→180B; AI networking $8→17→40→70B), AI rising to ~80% of revenue by 2028. Pro-forma EPS $7.18 (2025E) → $12.89 (2026E). GW deployed by client (2026/27/28E): Google 2.5/4.0/4.8, Anthropic 1.4/3.2/7.0, OpenAI 0/1.3/4.3, Meta 0/1.0/2.9 → total 4.1/9.9/19.6 GW; total AI rev $57B/$139B/$274B. (Capstone official model, 2026-06-10)
- **Bull:** FY28 is the real story. JPM Harlan Sur (2026-06-05 / 2026-06-17 calls): ~20–22GW deploy in FY28 → **$250–300B AI rev**, ~**$35–38 EPS** vs cons ~$25. TPU V8i "Sunfish" = ~70% of Google's FY27 TPU demand, ~6.5M units ≈ **~$100B** alone; +~$45B networking. The April **5-yr (through 2031) Google LTA** carries *rising guaranteed annual revenues* and spans next 4 TPU gens (V8–V11); V9 2nm (4 compute die / 16 HBM) "**no delay**," ramps C28. Citi (Malik) #1 semis pick 2026, PT $500, AI →~81% of sales by F4Q28 (briefing 2026-05-12); reaffirmed AVGO as #2 mega-cap in its 2026-06-29 memory-supply ranking (TPUs assumed ~85% memory-fulfilled). MS keeps AVGO OW within its top AI-semi picks (NVDA/AVGO/ALAB; MS Asia on AI Factories). Internal Capstone model (Felipe, 2026-06-08): 2026E rev $115B / 2027E $190B / 2028E $315B; Anthropic largest AI client by GW in 2028.
- **Bear:** (1) **FY27 not raised** despite better visibility → the binary debate (Street $116B vs buyside $150B+). (2) **GM compression** — ASIC mix drags consolidated GM toward low-70s; HBM pass-through pricing an added headwind, now quantified at ~$2.5k of a ~$12k TPU ASP (Jefferies Curtis, 2026-06-04; Fubon/Jefferies). (3) **Google diversification / TPU8 share loss** — Hock conceded "diversity of sources"; StoneX flags **TPU8 went to Alchip on 2nm/A16**, while AVGO retains TPU v1–7 (StoneX/John Su, 2025-08-20); the CLT/MediaTek "Zerafish" training chip also exists, though Sur argues AVGO keeps the higher-value inferencing roadmap and the COT chip "still needs optimization/respins." (4) **Power/datacenter readiness** is now the binding constraint on multi-GW greenfield ramps (SIG/Susquehanna callback, 2026-06-03) — Sur reads this as *why* Hock won't raise FY27. (5) Independent caution: EnerTuition "Competitive Risk Starting To Materialize" (2026-06-07). (6) **Crowding:** AVGO is the most crowded name in the AI/networking set (UBS 2026 overview, 2025-12-22). (7) **Multiple still de-rating + lumpier book (UBS Tim Rohde, 2026-06-04):** if forced to choose, Rohde **buys NVDA over AVGO** — AVGO's multiple "still has more to come down" (partly the Google/MediaTek share drumbeat that "only gets louder"), its book is lumpier and more concentrated (Google + a few labs), and it shows backend/substrate/packaging constraints NVDA doesn't ("Jensen drops $20B Vera out of thin air and gets supply"). (8) **Asia checks** flag the Broadcom chip still in development → V8 a 2026 event, the Anthropic rack order cut to ~$2-3B (internalized at GCP), and AVGO as a near-term "funding short" (Citi/Jefferies, 2026-05-22). (9) **New merchant-ASIC entrants:** LG Electronics is launching an external ASIC design-service business explicitly modeled on Broadcom and Marvell (@jukan05, 2026-06-30) — early-stage but a directional read that the design-service field is drawing more scaled entrants. (10) **Pumafish (TPU 9i) reportedly cancelled/postponed** — JPM (Gokul Hariharan, MediaTek note, 2026-07-01) reports Broadcom's competing "Pumafish" ASIC program cancelled or postponed, potentially ceding 2028-29 TPU-v9-generation share to MediaTek (though Broadcom still expected on another Google TPU v10 project alongside MediaTek's Icefish); this directly reverses the 2026-06-29 Jefferies framing that had Pumafish on-track (see Changelog). JPM/Hariharan also flags a possible fast-inference SRAM-based ASIC in Google's v9 family ("Merope") likely going to **Marvell rather than Broadcom** as Google's Compute program expands to match workloads with specific ASICs — a further competitive-share nuance against AVGO's custom-silicon franchise. (11) **UBS quantifies the share cede to MediaTek:** UBS (Sunny Lin, "Asia Semiconductors — Sector Keys", 2026-07-02) forecasts Broadcom shipping **91%/62% of TPUs in 2026/27E** as MediaTek rises **9%→38%**, on Google moving more front-end TPU design in-house and running dual Broadcom-v8i / MediaTek-v8t tracks into the v9 ramp in 2028 — a concrete sell-side unit-share model for the Google-diversification bear thesis (MediaTek's per-die service fee ~50% below Broadcom's). (12) **GS models a 50/50 Broadcom/MediaTek split on the next-gen Google TPU** — in its TSMC webinar GS frames the next-gen TPU share as an even split between the two design partners, flagged as *contested* since US investors still expect Broadcom to hold the higher share (Goldman Sachs "TSMC preview", 2026-07-09); adds another sell-side mark to the Redburn/Nomura/UBS Google→MediaTek share-cede thread.
- **Bull (2028 GW framing):** MS Joe Moore (via Skyler Risom sales note, 2026-06-30) forecasts AVGO ships **~14GW total compute in 2028**, with MS Internet's Brian Nowak estimating ~60% (~9GW) will be TPU, tied to Google's TPU retention/sell math — a data point in the same 2028 step-up debate as the Capstone house model's 19.6GW total and JPM Sur's ~20-22GW FY28 estimate above. MS's Joseph Moore (Semiconductor Inventory Tracker, 2026-06-30) separately prefers AVGO among compute/networking names on a tightening supply/inventory risk profile (alongside NVDA).
- **Bull (Apple joins the ASIC roster through 2031; JPM Harlan Sur, 2026-07-06):** JPM (reiterate Overweight, price $360.45) reads AVGO's **extended Apple technology agreement through 2031** — widening from RF-only to **custom ASICs across multiple product generations** — as fresh evidence of ASIC dominance. On top of the 10+ yr Apple client-ASIC history (touch-controller, wireless-charging ASICs), JPM believes AVGO **recently won a new Apple power-management ASIC** (starting next year), is **co-designing an AI/datacenter server CPU with Apple**, and expects an eventual **full AI XPU ASIC** for Apple. The multi-year ASIC agreements now span **Google, OpenAI, Meta, and Apple** — JPM's core bull framing that the market underestimates AVGO's 30+ yr ASIC leadership. Corroborates the Jefferies (2026-06-29) roadmap read that one of AVGO's "two additional customers" is Apple (Baltra server CPU). (JPM · Harlan Sur, 2026-07-06)
- **Bull (JPM investor meeting reaffirms AI compute + networking runway; JPM · Harlan Sur, 2026-07-08):** reiterate Overweight after hosting Hock Tan / CFO Amie Thuener / IR Ji Yoo (AVGO $370.78 on 07-07). Key rebuttals to the bear threads: inference demand pulling custom-XPU adoption forward (**~50/50 XPU/GPU unit mix** at the largest frontier-model builders next year), customers pulling in next-gen XPU tape-outs, Jalapeño validating ASIC execution (design→tape-out ~9 mo) with a next-gen OpenAI XPU taping out "very soon," **TPU v9 on track to ramp CY28** (400G SerDes already developed), Tomahawk 6 sold out, Apple extended through 2031 (RF+ASICs, likely an AI XPU), COT fears overstated (multi-die/packaging/IP complexity), and management committed to holding **ASIC margins in-target** despite wafer/substrate/HBM cost inflation — directly countering the COT/share-loss and margin-compression bears. NB: JPM Sur's "v9 on track CY28" conflicts with JPM Hariharan's 2026-07-01 "Pumafish cancelled/postponed" report (see Bear item 10) — an unresolved intra-house tension on v9-generation share. (JPM · Harlan Sur, 2026-07-08)
- **Bull (Rothschild/Redburn reiterate Buy on the Apple deal; Rowan Adley, 2026-07-09):** Rothschild & Co / Redburn reiterates AVGO **Buy** on the Apple contract-extension detail (**>$30bn** through 2031) — another sell-side reaffirmation alongside Citi (2026-07-08, Buy $500) that the expanded Apple ASIC agreement is a net positive for the custom-silicon narrative. (Rothschild & Co / Redburn · Rowan Adley, 2026-07-09)
- **Bull (MS explicitly defends AVGO's ~80% ASIC share — the debate is now two-sided; Morgan Stanley · Joseph Moore "In Defense of AVGO's ASIC Share", reiterate Overweight PT $502, 2026-07-14):** the first direct sell-side rebuttal of the Google→MediaTek share-cede bear thread (Bear items 10-12). MS argues MediaTek TPU participation is **real but displacement fears are premature**, expecting **AVGO to retain ~80% TPU share over time** and calling "50% share / eventual displacement" scenarios too draconian; the **moat is HBM supply + packaging execution + scale**, which make rapid Broadcom displacement unlikely. MS frames AVGO a **core AI winner and close #2 to NVDA** (ASIC leadership + networking + new-customer ramps), and notes AVGO/NVDA have **lagged other AI semis YTD** precisely because of this TPU-share overhang, as investor preference rotates to "growthier bottleneck" AI names — an analogy to last year's MRVL/Alchip Trainium-share debate, where "fully replaced" proved too draconian even though Marvell did lose some share. Independently corroborated by @BenBajarin (2026-07-14): the ASIC-share concern "has never come up… I'd estimate it to remain even higher than 80%" — MediaTek likely doubles its business by end-decade but AVGO's revenue line keeps rising. Reframes the Bear items 10-12 (JPM Hariharan Pumafish cancel, GS 50/50 07-09, UBS 91%/62% 07-02) as an active two-way debate, not a one-way share-loss narrative. NB the countervailing @jukan05 (07-15) datapoint — Samsung weighing back-end design of the I/O die for Google's 2nm TPU v10 "Icefish" co-designed with MediaTek — keeps the next-gen (v10) MediaTek-role risk live. (Morgan Stanley / Joseph Moore, 2026-07-14)
- **Bear (HBM-in-BOM = the Google→MediaTek share driver; Redburn "Conor O'Mara Asia Trip Takeaways", 2026-06-23):** the key reason Google is shifting TPU share to MediaTek is that **Broadcom charges the hyperscaler for HBM inside the BOM whereas MediaTek does not** — so Google can cut a "gigantic part of the bill of materials" by moving to MediaTek, as long as MediaTek stays as good as Broadcom (so far it is). Conor frames Broadcom's Google market share going **6% (2026) → 30% (2028)** [i.e. MediaTek's gain at Broadcom's expense], with **400G switch timing (V9 vs V10 at Google) the key swing factor** — Broadcom is currently the only 400G supplier, so if Google adopts 400G at V9 it could cap MediaTek's gains; Conor/Sherman's checks lean toward Google adopting 400G only at **V10** (late). Separately, Sherman **brought down some Broadcom CoWoS** on delays to the OpenAI and Meta projects (mass production slips to 2H, small '27 contribution) (Redburn "Conor O'Mara Asia Trip Takeaways", 2026-06-23). Corroborated by StoneX/TrendForce: **Google previously relied entirely on Broadcom for HBM** procurement, but MediaTek (TPU V8/V9) is now increasingly handling Google's HBM directly and is in talks with Samsung — confirming the HBM-procurement leakage away from Broadcom (StoneX/TrendForce "Memory expert call — Avril", 2026-06-23).
- **Bear (MediaTek wins Google TPU v9 primary orders on SerDes timing; FinTwit, 2026-06-24):** MediaTek won primary production orders for Google's **TPU v9** chips by delivering a custom **336G SerDes** solution after Broadcom's more ambitious **448G SerDes missed the production deadline**. This is a concrete, supply-chain-verified data point that Broadcom failed to meet Google's timeline on a key technical deliverable — reinforcing the Conor/Redburn HBM-in-BOM narrative with a second dimension (SerDes execution). A GOOGL employee interview (via @RihardJarc) argued that the **real value of ASIC designers like AVGO is now their TSM CoWoS allocation, not the co-design** — a narratively bearish reframe for AVGO's "design premium = higher ASP" thesis (@dnystedt; @RihardJarc, 2026-06-24).
- **Bull (Arete rolls to FY28E, raises PT to $530; Arete "Real-Time Views #68", 2026-07-21):** Arete (Janco Venter) reiterate **Buy**, PT **$448 → $530** (rolled to FY28E, 40% upside from $378). New model: **AI sales $57.2bn (+182%) FY26 / $128bn (+123%) FY27 / $177bn (+39%) FY28** → **FY28E group revenue $239bn (+30% yoy)**; **FY28E non-GAAP EPS $26.79 (from $24.45 prior)**, FY26E $11.68 / FY27E $20.47; non-GAAP EBIT margin held ~66-67% across the window. PT = avg of SOTP (Semis FY28E EBIT $125.1bn × 18.2x peer EV/EBIT; Software $33.6bn × 11x), SOX-multiple (20.4x on FY28E EPS), and DCF (WACC 13.9% vs 12.3% prior, 40% '25-30 rev CAGR). Arete's $128bn FY27 AI number sits above the bear "Street $116B" bogey but below the buy-side $150B+ — and its FY28E $177bn AI / $239bn total is a fresh Street-high datapoint on the FY28 step-up debate. (Arete Research "Real-Time Views #68", 2026-07-21)
- **Bear (Meta pursuing customer-owned tooling to bypass Broadcom; SemiAnalysis "Meta Infrastructure Needs A Culture Reset", 2026-07-21):** Meta's **>$2.5bn Rivos acquisition** (2025) gave it the ability to **bypass partners like Broadcom to manage manufacturing/testing of its own custom chips (customer-owned tooling, COT)** — a structural negative for AVGO's Meta custom-silicon (MTIA) share. **Heavily hedged, though:** SemiAnalysis frames the Rivos deal itself as a misstep (a COT team could be built for ~$100m/yr vs $2.5bn paid), ~30% of Rivos engineers already laid off, the Rivos-based "Olympus" chip cancelled, Meta reverting to its existing MTIA architecture through MTIA 600, and the follow-on Rivos-tech "Phoebe" chip not taping out until 2028 (some inside Meta think it too could be cancelled). Net read: the COT/bypass ambition is real and directionally negative for AVGO's Meta share, but Meta's execution is troubled enough that near-term displacement risk is low — consistent with Hock's "COT not a chance" framing (07-07 JPM call). (SemiAnalysis "Meta Infrastructure Needs A Culture Reset", 2026-07-21)
- **Bull (the Samsung MOU is the supply answer to the FY28 step-up; JPM · Harlan Sur, 2026-07-27; UBS · Timothy Arcuri, 2026-07-27):** the 07-25 Samsung MOU got its first sell-side quantification. JPM reiterates **OW, PT $580** and frames the **>$200B of Samsung memory/foundry through 2030** as implying **>$1T of cumulative AVGO AI revenue (ASICs + networking) over 2026-2030 — a 60%+ CAGR on forward AI revenue, still >20%/yr after 2028**. Note the direction of that inference: JPM is **back-solving AI revenue from a disclosed memory-purchase commitment**, not the other way round, so it is a *consistency check* on the FY28 bull case (Capstone house $274B AI rev in 2028; Barclays >$300B; Arete $177B) rather than independent evidence — but it is the first datapoint anchored in a *contracted input purchase* rather than a GW forecast. The mix matters for how much is logic: JPM puts it at **90-95% HBM / 5-10% foundry wafers**, with the ~$40-50B foundry leg going to **networking/broadband/telco silicon, not the leading-edge XPUs**. UBS adds that the deal buys **Samsung 2nm access + HBM + advanced packaging** (packaging folded into AVGO's own Singapore site) and **potentially reduces TSMC reliance**. **The genuinely new risk this creates is the mirror image of the bull case:** Sur says Samsung will supply **~75-85% of all AVGO HBM requirements going forward** — supply is secured, but single-sourced, at a moment when memory is the binding constraint for the whole complex (Citi's 06-29 "DRAM supply is the new ranking metric"). Bernstein (Li/Rasgon, 07-27) is the check on over-reading it: the announcement **does not disclose whether AVGO will produce AI ASIC at Samsung for wafer or for advanced packaging**, and they judge the **[[TSM]] impact negligible** — so this is not yet evidence of foundry share moving.
- **Bull (UBS rebuts the MediaTek share-cede on execution risk, not share math; UBS · Timothy Arcuri, 2026-07-27):** UBS argues **investors have become too negative on the Google situation** — Google's choice of a **chiplet design for v8t de-risks AVGO's ramp versus MediaTek**, which is **heavily reliant on INTC's EMIB-T process, which UBS thinks still suffers from very low yields and immaturity**. This is a different line of defence than MS's 07-14 "~80% share by ASP/mix" argument (see above): it attacks the *deliverability* of MediaTek's v9-generation win rather than its size. Both now sit against Bear items 10-12 (JPM Hariharan Pumafish-cancelled, GS 50/50, UBS Sunny Lin 91%/62%) — note the intra-UBS tension: **Arcuri (US) is defending AVGO's Google position while Sunny Lin (Asia) models AVGO's TPU unit share falling 91%→62% in 2026/27E**. Unresolved; flagged rather than netted. (UBS · Timothy Arcuri, 2026-07-27)
- **Where the sell-side stands:** Unanimous Buy/OW post-print, PT range **$485–$580**. JPM (Sur) OW **$580**; Barclays (O'Malley) OW $500 (desk Rand: AVGO a "funding short" near-term); Bernstein (Rasgon) OP $550, cut multiple 30x→25x; MS (Moore) OW $502 (prefers NVDA); Jefferies (Curtis) Buy $550; DB (Seymore) Buy $515; UBS (Arcuri) Buy $485 (UBS 2026 overview, 2025-12-22 had PT $475 — since raised; **re-confirmed unchanged in the Figure-1 valuation table and the disclosure section of UBS's 2026-07-27 advanced-packaging deep dive — Buy, PT US$485 against a US$392 share price (US$392.47 priced 23 Jul 2026), mkt cap US$1,881,276m, 2026/27E P/E 32.8x/18.1x, P/BV 17.4x/10.8x, ROE 60.6%/74.0%** — UBS · Sunny Lin / Randy Abrams / Nicolas Gaudois / Timothy Arcuri, "Can Intel challenge TSMC's advanced packaging leadership?", 2026-07-27); Citi (Malik) Buy $500 (AVGO #2 mega-cap in its 2026-06-29 memory-supply ranking, behind NVDA). **Arete (Janco Venter) Buy, PT raised to $530 (from $448, rolled to FY28E, 40% upside)** — see the 07-21 estimate detail below. FinTwit @ParadisLabs (2026-06-15) notes a de-rate to **16.2x 2028E**, planning to add.
- **Popular-press valuation framing (Barron's, 2026-06-01):** semiconductor revenue seen at **$132B FY2027**, implying a **2027 P/E of 23x** and **PEG of 0.37x** — a mainstream-press datapoint (not sell-side) that the growth-adjusted multiple screens cheap against the AI-revenue ramp; consistent with the FinTwit de-rate note above. Barron's "Top CEOs of 2026" (2026-06-22) frames the ASIC-vs-merchant-GPU debate directly: AVGO's custom ASICs let hyperscalers "use Tan's ASICs as a scalpel, rather than turn to the Swiss Army knife of Nvidia's general-purpose chips" — a bull framing for AVGO's differentiated position vs NVDA in the sell-side "which do you own" debate (Barclays/MS/UBS all flag some NVDA-vs-AVGO preference above).

## Catalysts / what to watch
- **🆕🔴🔴 Q3 FY26 PRINT — WEDNESDAY 2026-09-02, ~4:15pm ET release / 5:00pm ET call. Implied move 6.3%; JPM positioning score +3 (−5/+5).** The four numbers that decide it, in order: **(1) the FY27 AI revenue guide** — buyside landing zone **$120bn**, several respondents at **$130bn**, Jefferies says the bar is already **$130-140bn**, and *"some expectations assume more than $150bn"* (MS). **(2) the October AI revenue guide** — MS models **$21.2bn (+32% q/q)** vs **$16.0bn** in July; Jefferies models total October revenue **$35.5bn (+20% q/q)** vs Street $34.7bn. **(3) whether management quantifies FY28 at all** — JPM does *"not expect management to provide a formal FY28 AI revenue target."* **(4) the first call with new CFO Amie Thuener**, a former [[GOOG]] executive. ⚠️ **The Jefferies supply constraint is the falsifier to hold in mind: if C27 AI revenue is capped by wafers/packaging/HBM rather than orders, a raise above ~$130bn is not available even with a >$150bn backlog.** (JPM Sales · Meyers 08-31 · MS · Moore 08-31 · Jefferies · Curtis 08-28)
- **🆕 POST-PRINT BROKER CALLBACKS ALREADY SCHEDULED — the fastest read on how the Street re-rates the guide.** **Morgan Stanley Q3 group callback with Joseph Moore, 2026-09-03, 9:00am ET** (stream); **JPM "Ask Me Anything" on AVGO earnings with Harlan Sur, Thursday 2026-09-03, 10:00am ET** (webinar registration via JPM). (MS TMT desk 08-31 · JPM Sales · Meyers 08-31)
- **🆕 VMware Explore 2026 — Day 1 keynote MONDAY 2026-08-31, 7:00pm ET.** The software leg of the story gets its annual airing 48 hours before the semis print; watch for any restatement of the VMware/VCF subscription-conversion progress that the infrastructure-software segment guide rests on. (MS TMT desk, 2026-08-31)
- **🆕 THE V10 AWARD DECISION IS NOW DATED: "the next six months," i.e. by ~FEB 2027 (JPM Asia · Gokul Hariharan, 2026-08-31).** *"We expect the final project award in the next six months, which should clarify Mediatek's involvement and reduce the uncertainty currently priced into the stock"* — [[GOOG]] *"appears to have approached ALL design-service vendors at the RFQ stage to propose a disaggregated, full CoT model."* ➜ **This is the event that adjudicates the ~80%-share defence (MS) against the majority-unit-share claim (JPM Asia). Falsifier for the AVGO bull case: an award in which AVGO is named on neither the compute die nor the I/O/packaging integration.** ⚠️ **JPM's own base case is that the v10 model stays *"similar to v9"* — MediaTek keeping SerDes IP / I-O die / packaging / program management, GOOGL designing the compute die in-house, back-end farmed to GUC or Alchip. AVGO is not in that base case at all, which is the uncomfortable half.**
- **🆕 BERNSTEIN 8th ANNUAL SEMIS BUS TOUR, 2026-09-15/16, San Jose — AVGO on the roster alongside [[ALAB]], [[AMAT]], AMBA, LSCC, MPWR, [[NVDA]], [[NXPI]].** First scheduled corporate-access window after the print. (Bernstein · Tyler Seidman, 2026-08-31)

- **🆕🔴 2026-09-02 — FQ3 FY26 print, and the bar is set by consensus rather than by the guide: ~$120bn of FY27 AI sales is in the Street's numbers versus management's ">$100bn" (BofA · Arya, 2026-08-19).** That ~20% gap is the scoreable item. BofA expects reiteration of the custom trajectory plus the **[[META]] and [[OPENAI]] ramps (each ~1GW at >$10bn) starting CY27**.
- **🆕🔴 Google supplier-base broadening — the live competitive question, now with three named challengers.** [[MRVL]] signed an expanded Google agreement on 2026-08-19 (attach content, warrant to ~$120bn cumulative through Jan-2033); [[MEDIATEK]] is credited with 20-30% share on cost-optimised variants; and [[AMD]] is now reported in **TPU v10**. **The falsifier for the "core die is safe" thesis is specific: AVGO's Google LTA runs through CY31, so watch for any Google disclosure or supply-chain report placing a NON-Broadcom designer on a core TPU compute die, as distinct from attach silicon.**
- **CEO/CFO Virtual Group NDR: Tuesday, July 7, 2026** (JPM / Mark Schilsky desk, 2026-06-30) — watch for commentary on the 2028 GW/TPU-mix debate and the Pumafish status reversal (JPM Hariharan, 2026-07-01). **Readout (JPM · Harlan Sur, 2026-07-08):** reiterate Overweight — ~50/50 XPU/GPU unit mix next year, TPU v9 on track to ramp CY28 (400G SerDes working; note tension w/ Hariharan's 07-01 Pumafish-cancelled read), Tomahawk 6 sold out, Apple extended through 2031 (RF+ASICs, likely AI XPU), ASIC margins held in-target.
- **Q3 FY26 print: Wednesday, Sep 2, 2026** AMC (call 2pm PT) — watch AI-semi print vs $16B guide, GM trajectory, and any FY27 AI raise.
- **🗓️🔴 THE SEPTEMBER PRINT IS NOW ALSO A NAMED CREDIT-DESK EVENT: *"For Single Name, ORCL and AVGO remain front and center ahead of their respective September [reporting dates]"*** (Morgan Stanley Global Credit Strategy & Securitized Products, "AI Debt Financing Tracker: The Summer Spreads Turned Wider", 2026-08-20). ⚠️ **Relevant to this page specifically because of the contingent exposure in `## Risks`: the backstop is drawn in ~16 stages with ~$24bn expected paid out by summer 2027, so the cost and availability of the private/structured chip-financing channel is a live input rather than macro colour. Context MS attaches: ~$445bn issued across credit channels YTD vs $217bn for FULL-YEAR 2025 (FY26E $500–550bn), Semiconductors at $42bn YTD (+57%), a brand-new 4(a)(2) private-placement "chip financing" channel at 0 → $35bn, and MS guiding to "a busy fall and continued spread widening." Offsetting: the [[NVDA]]+AVGO credit complex widened only ~20bp YTD versus +26/+29bp for the hyperscaler buckets — the market is charging the chip vendors less than the buyers of their chips.**
- **🔴 TPU V10 (~two years out) is the next competitive checkpoint at [[GOOG]], and BofA reports it as an open contest rather than a settled one: an active debate over whether [[AMD]], BROADCOM, Alchip or GUC penetrates the TPU supply chain at V10, against MediaTek's own claim to have been on V10's "triplet" architecture with Google since 2H last year and to own the integration** (BofA Asia semis + components call, 2026-08-20). ⚠️ **Two-sided: Broadcom is still a named candidate two generations past the v9 order loss (incumbency not conceded), but it is now one of four contenders at a customer where it was the default, and the dated head start belongs to MediaTek. Do not read "V10" as a design-win claim — it is a debate about what happens ~2 years out, sourced from a broker-hosted call with no AVGO rating attached.**
- Sunfish/V8i volume ramp (shipping ~now), Tomahawk 6 (102T) ramp into Oct-Q (margin-accretive), 200T switch tape-out this quarter.
- First $35B Apollo SPV tranche execution; Anthropic FY27 ~3.5GW, OpenAI 1.3GW, Meta 1GW initial (deliveries 2H FY27). Note OpenAI's announced ~26GW of compute deals spread across NVDA/AVGO/AMD, with some overlap (UBS 2026 overview, 2025-12-22).
- Advanced packaging: Singapore substrate ramps Aug; in-house advanced packaging (Applied Materials Singapore) 2H FY27 (Sur, 2026-06-05); ASE FoCoS offload of networking processors ramping mid-FY26 (Fubon/Jefferies).

- Scale-up Ethernet (SUE) adoption vs UALink; Tomahawk 6 XPU scale-up design wins; scale-up CPO decisions into 2028.
- V9 2nm milestones / any COT (MediaTek) progress signals on Google share; TPU8 (Alchip) execution as a share-loss tell.

- CEO pay-package milestone: Hock Tan's **1.8M-share award (~$750M)** vests on AVGO AI revenue reaching **$120B by end-FY2030** (Barron's, 2026-06-01) — a management-incentive marker for the multi-year AI ramp.

## Risks
- 🔴 **CONTINGENT, OFF-BALANCE-SHEET CREDIT EXPOSURE TIED TO THE ASIC FRANCHISE — now corroborated by an independent reconstruction, not just modelled.** AVGO provides a credit backstop on **$30bn of the $34.5bn of debt financing more than 1 GW of Google TPU systems leased to [[ANTHROPIC]]**, with a **reported maximum exposure of $29bn**; the senior **A1 ($6bn, T+1pp) and A2 ($24bn, 5.75%)** tranches are backstopped, the junior **B ($4.5bn, 8.5%)** is not. On an Anthropic default AVGO **can take over the five-year lease or arrange a rack sale, then covers remaining A1/A2 shortfalls** — i.e. **second-loss against used-TPU-rack residual value, which is the unmodelled input.** Exposure is **drawn in ~16 stages, ~$24bn expected paid out by summer 2027**, so it **rises into mid-2027 before amortising** — today's drawn balance is not the peak. AVGO does **not** fund the $30bn in cash; it lends its credit ("vendor-supported financing"). (Epoch AI · Campbell Hutcheson, 2026-08-12; reconciles with JPM "AI Capex 2.0", 2026-06-16 and AVGO's 10-Q.) ⚠️ **The 2.75pp A2-vs-B spread is an UPPER BOUND on what the backstop is worth to lenders (it also embeds seniority) — not a price for the guarantee.**
  - **🆕🔴 THE FUNDING CHANNEL BEHIND THIS KIND OF STRUCTURE WENT FROM ZERO TO $35BN IN ONE YEAR, AND IT IS THE STANDOUT LINE IN MORGAN STANLEY'S CREDIT TABLE (MS US Credit Strategy · Vishwas Patkar / Fernanda Lima / Vishwanath Tirupattur / Carolyn Campbell / Eva Baurmeister / Christina Sigler, "A Break from the Heat, For Now", 2026-08-14).** Issuance by channel, YTD prior-year → YTD current (% change): **US HY 15 → 41 (+176%) · US leveraged loans 2 → 10 (+394%) · non-USD IG hyperscalers 15 → 62 (+313%) · EUR HY ([[CRWV]]) 0 → 2 · TOTAL 189 → 382 (+102%) · "CHIP FINANCING" VIA 4(a)(2) PRIVATE PLACEMENTS 0 → 35.** ➜ **A brand-new, $35bn-and-counting private-placement channel dedicated to chip financing is the market infrastructure that makes vendor-supported structures like the $30bn Anthropic/TPU backstop repeatable rather than exceptional. That is two-sided for this page: it is how the ASIC franchise gets financed at scale (constructive for volume), and it is how much contingent, off-public-market exposure the complex is accumulating (the risk this bullet exists to track).** **MS's own framing points the same way — financing should shift *"increasingly toward chips and components, with a larger role for private capital and high-quality credit support,"* with balance-sheet and market capacity ample but increasingly price-sensitive, *"making wider spreads the natural release valve"*; MS favours COLLATERAL-BACKED risk.** ⚠️ **The spread context is the reassuring half, and it should be quoted with the number: YTD spread change (bp) by bucket — HQ Hyperscalers +29 · Hyperscalers incl. [[ORCL]] and [[SPCX]] +26 · **[[NVDA]]+BROADCOM +20** · Semis +2 · Tech +11 · IG +1. The NVDA/AVGO credit complex widened 20bp YTD, i.e. LESS than the hyperscalers — the credit market is differentiating inside tech and is charging the chip vendors less than the buyers of their chips.** ⚠️ **None of this is an AVGO-specific spread, rating or estimate: MS's credit desk publishes no AVGO mark, and the 4(a)(2) figure is an industry channel total, not Broadcom issuance.**
  - **🗓️🔴 AND ONE DATED WATCH FLAG FROM THE FOLLOW-UP TRACKER: *"For Single Name, ORCL and AVGO remain front and center ahead of their respective September [reporting dates]"* (MS Global Credit Strategy & Securitized Products · Lima / Campbell / Tirupattur / Patkar / Egan / Becker / Loke / Verma / Tyler / Konetzke, "AI Debt Financing Tracker: The Summer Spreads Turned Wider", 2026-08-20).** ➜ **The FQ3 print on 2026-09-02 is now a credit-desk event as well as an equity one, and it lands into a market MS expects to keep widening (*"a busy fall and continued spread widening"*; ~$445bn issued across credit channels YTD versus $217bn for FULL-YEAR 2025, FY26E $500–550bn; Semiconductors specifically at **$42bn YTD, +57%**, having run 5, 0, 0, 8, 0, 25, 0, 5 Jan→Aug).** ⚠️ **Why it matters for THIS bullet rather than as generic macro: Broadcom's contingent exposure is drawn in ~16 stages with ~$24bn expected paid out by summer 2027, so the cost and availability of the private/structured channel between now and then is an input to the franchise, not a backdrop.**
  - **🆕🔴🔴 THE SPECIALIST CREDIT DESK NOW SIZES THE EXPOSURE AT ~$290–480bn, AND IT CLOSED ITS BONDS ON IT (Morgan Stanley TMT **Credit** Research · Lindsay A Tyler, with Nishant Satyam — "AVGO Credit: More Compute, More Contingency?", 2026-06-19, and "AVGO Credit: TPUs, Take Two?", 2026-08-05).** ⚠️ **CREDIT research: no equity rating, no equity PT, and MS discloses it is a paid advisor to Broadcom on the AI XPV Platform. Nothing here supersedes an equity mark on this page.** The arithmetic behind the number this bullet has been carrying only qualitatively: **the $35bn deal / $29bn max RVS supports 1.2–1.5GW ⇒ $19.3–24.2bn of maximum RVS exposure PER GW ⇒ ~$290–480bn of theoretical maximum exposure if replicated across the >20GW AI XPV platform** (MS headline ~$350bn on 06-19; by 08-05, with a second tranche reported and ~17GW still implied, *"AVGO's theoretical maximum supported exposure could surpass ~$300bn"*, while the ~$480bn top end *"now appears punitive following APCHIP amortization disclosures"*). **Treated as debt, that scenario puts gross adjusted leverage at 3.3x by FY28E versus Moody's 2.5x gross downgrade threshold** (net adjusted stays at 1.8x versus S&P's 3x) — so in MS's own framing *"the issue therefore would more be a hypothetical capital call, rather than the ratings impact."* 🔴 **The structural point to hold onto is not the leverage table but the COLLATERAL: the tail risk is *"heightened by the lack of an established TPU/XPU secondary market. TPUs appear less fungible and liquid than GPUs, with third-party sales only recently disclosed by [[GOOG]]."*** ➜ **AVGO's backstop is second-loss against the residual value of used TPU racks in a market that does not yet exist — which is why the same desk that recommended AVGO bonds on 05-29 ("a potential entry point") had closed every one of those recommendations by 06-19 and, by 08-05, called the level "not … a compelling re-entry point", with AVGO 10Y in line with the BBB index and Aa1/AA [[NVDA]] only ~20bp tighter.** ⚠️ **Two-sided, and the offsetting half must travel with it: the same desk sizes the OPPORTUNITY — AVGO's AI XPV alone at 20GW could drive *"an additional ~$430-550bn of TPU/XPU financing beyond the first tranche"* — and the mitigants are real (S&P leverage near 1x against a 3x threshold; >$5bn of planned debt reduction; execution constraints on power, labour and supply chain slowing the path to 20GW; and risk-sharing with Apollo and Blackstone Credit plus possible [[GOOG]] shell-lease backstops given its ratings capacity).**
- **Customer concentration:** top-five end customers a significant share of net revenue (FY25 10-K); AI rev increasingly tied to a handful of frontier labs — Sur flags AVGO is *more* exposed to OpenAI/Anthropic frontier-lab risk than NVDA (2026-06-17).
- **Supply chain:** dependence on a limited number of contract manufacturers/foundries (TSMC), HBM and substrate (FY25 10-K); back-end packaging cited as the FY27 supply constraint (UBS Arcuri, 2026-06-04). AVGO's stickiness as a quasi-foundry to hyperscalers cuts both ways — exposes it to price pressure once CoWoS capacity eases (StoneX/John Su, 2025-08-20). Citi's "DRAM Supply Is the New Ranking Metric" (2026-06-29) assumes TPUs ~85% memory-fulfilled — memory allocation an emerging gate even for the #2-ranked mega-cap.
- **Margin mix:** structural GM dilution as low-margin ASICs scale faster than software/networking; HBM pass-through (~$2.5k/TPU) a quantified drag (Fubon/Jefferies).
- **Share loss / COT:** Google CLT-team (MediaTek) optionality; TPU8 routed to Alchip (StoneX/John Su, 2025-08-20); longer-term COT-model bypass risk (Jukan, briefing 2026-05-18).
- **Macro/AI-capex cyclicality & power availability** gating multi-GW deployments; geopolitical/China (InP, export controls).

If that is right, a bigger guide is not AVAILABLE to management even where the backlog supports it — which is the mechanism that reconciles Harlan's *">$150bn F27 backlog"* with a guide that lands at $120-130bn, and it is the single best explanation on this page for why a beat-and-raise can still sell off.** **On competition: *"we expect AVGO to be the dominant supplier into the TPU ecosystem but **the chatter related to MTK, AMD, and now MRVL are hard to disprove**"* — a materially weaker defence than MS's ~80% or JPM's contractual anchor, and logged as the softest of the three. Upside is dated to C28 *"alongside the deployment of the XPV"*, with *"many moving pieces (memory pricing, supply constraints, etc.)"* making it *"too early for management to frame that opportunity."* Also notes this is **the first earnings call with new CFO Amie Thuener**.** (Jefferies · Blayne Curtis, 2026-08-28) |

The **$500** in the alert body is treated as an error in the alert body and is retained in `## Changelog`. ⚠️ Still a sales relay of a research PT, not the research PDF.** | **competicao / demanda / guidance** | **bull** | ★ 🔴🔴 **THE MOST DIRECT REBUTTAL YET OF THE [[GOOG]]-SHARE-LOSS BEAR, AND IT REFRAMES THE [[MRVL]] DEAL AS ADDITIVE RATHER THAN DISPLACING: *"our channel checks over the last 90 days lead us to believe that **investor concerns around AVGO's competitive position at GOOGL are OVERSTATED**. JPM's evidence says the COT team stumbled. Both retained, not netted.**
🔴 **THE CONTRACTUAL ANCHOR, WHICH IS THE HARDEST FACT IN THE NOTE: *"we do not see any company displacing AVGO's position as GOOGL's core TPU partner, especially given the terms of the **5-year GOOGL-AVGO agreement signed in early April**, in which **GOOGL committed to ANNUALLY INCREASING TPU-related purchases at AVGO** and **anchored AVGO as the VOLUME PARTNER FOR THE NEXT 4 GENERATIONS OF TPU SKUs (v8-v11)**."*** ➜ **If the v8-v11 volume anchor is right, the Marvell warrant cannot be a core-TPU displacement inside that window by construction — which is exactly what the Barclays 08-27 MRVL note implied when it guessed the already-embedded Google revenue *"is CXL."***
🔴 **THE NUMBERS, AND THE FY27 CALL IS FAR ABOVE MANAGEMENT'S OWN FRAMEWORK: JPM models **$56B+ of AI revenue in FY26** and **2-2.5x y/y growth in FY27 — *"in the $130B+ range even when taking into account tight industry supply dynamics"*** — against management's reiterated **>$100B** framework. ➜ **A ~30% gap between the house model and the company's own guided framework is the single biggest number on this page and it is the whole setup: JPM expects management *"to either raise or provide stronger qualitative support"* for FY27 AI revenue at the print.** **Drivers named: expanding ASIC/XPU ramps across **GOOGL, [[META]], OAI, [[ANTHROPIC]], ByteDance/Alibaba**, plus AI networking on the **Tomahawk 6 (102T)** cycle. Near-term: July-quarter strength on the **initial TPU v8i ramp**, continued **TPU v7**, the **initial ramp of Meta's MTIA "Iris" ASIC**, and Tomahawk 5 — with October guidance implying **FY26 AI revenue >$56B** and the **initial volume ramp of [[OPENAI]]'s "Jalapeno" 1st-gen XPU ASIC**.**
🔴 **THE SUPPLY DATAPOINT THAT MATTERS MORE THAN THE FORECAST: *"**Tomahawk 5 demand still EXCEEDING SUPPLY** and next-gen **Tomahawk 6 effectively SOLD OUT THROUGH NEXT YEAR**."*** ➜ **A sold-out networking book through CY27 is a harder constraint-and-visibility statement than any revenue framework, and it is the piece of the FY27 call that does not depend on hyperscaler ramp timing.**
**FY28, framed as commentary rather than a target: JPM does *"not expect management to provide a formal FY28 AI revenue target"* but expects strongly constructive tone, supported by **GOOGL TPU v9 2nm ramping early CY28**, **incremental [[ANTHROPIC]] TPU demand**, **META MTIA expansion**, **OAI Jalapeno 1st/2nd-gen ASICs**, and ByteDance/Alibaba.** ⚠️ **Context for the whole note — the reason it exists: *"AVGO shares have LAGGED the broader semiconductor group YTD (SOX +69% vs. AVGO +7%)"*, which JPM attributes to the GOOGL share-loss fear plus the absence of incremental FY27 colour. This is a mean-reversion argument as much as a fundamental one.** |

| **08-25** | 🔴🔴 **SemiAnalysis · Bryan Shan / Myron Xie / Jordan Nanos / Wega Chu / Clara Ee / **Dylan Patel** — "OpenAI Jalapeño: Better Than Nvidia Blackwell?"** (**independent research, NOT a broker — no rating, no PT, no estimate**) | produto / competicao | **bull** | 🔴 **THE FULLEST THIRD-PARTY VALIDATION THE OPENAI XPU PROGRAM HAS EVER HAD, AND AVGO IS NAMED AS THE PARTNER TWICE OVER — silicon *and* switching.** SemiAnalysis was invited into OpenAI's labs and benchmarked the part with its InferenceX suite. **The chip:** unveiled in June *"in partnership with Broadcom, built from a blank slate exclusively for LLM inference"*; design work began mid-2024, **tape-out (of the CoWoS design, not just the top die) in November 2025** — *"an extremely fast ASIC development cycle"* of ~16 months. All published results are on **A0 silicon 9 months into the program**; a **B0 stepping is already in the fab with *"roughly a 25% perf-per-watt improvement"***, delivering **13.4 PFLOPs MXFP4 on a single reticle-sized N3P compute die at 700W TDP** (vs **17.5 PFLOPs of dense NVFP4 for a similar-size Rubin compute die on the same node at 900–1,150W**). **HBM4 at 15.4TB/s per package** — *"implies its HBM4 can hit 10Gbps pin speeds, which would give it a slight edge over the 9.6Gbps Nvidia is getting out of its HBM4 in Rubin"*; *"The HBM is likely provided by [[SAMSUNG]]."* 🔴 **THE AVGO NETWORKING CONTENT, WHICH IS THE PART THIS PAGE HAS BEEN MISSING — the scale-up fabric is Broadcom silicon end to end: each rack carries 8 "Chana" switch trays, *"six … for the local domain, which come with one 102.4T Tomahawk 6 switch ASIC each"*, plus 2 global trays that SemiAnalysis thinks could be *"2x 102.4T Tomahawk 6 switches making up to 204.8T per switch tray"*.** Rack topology: 16 "Vindaloo" trays × 8 ASICs = **128 Jalapeño per rack**, local all-to-all over a copper backplane *"just like that of Nvidia's Oberon"*, global domain of **2,048 XPUs across 16 racks** (copper backplane + 1.6T transceivers + optical circuit switch, 8 rails); **~160kW for the two-rack (host + ASIC) system, "basically a double-wide GB300 rack"**; system-level design *"done in partnership with Celestica"*; host "Katsu" trays run **2× [[AMD]] Turin-class EPYC + 1.5TB DRAM**. **Timing: *"production is currently scheduled to gradually ramp over 2027 with most of the output currently scheduled for Q4 of next year"*; *"The next goal is 100MW."*** ⚠️ **THE OFFSET, RECORDED BECAUSE IT IS THE MARGIN ARGUMENT AND IT COMES FROM A BULL: on perf/TCO Jalapeño and Vera Rubin are *"head-to-head, producing almost the same number of output tokens per $"*, and SemiAnalysis attributes part of that to price rather than physics — *"part of this TCO advantage comes from trading Nvidia's high margins for Broadcom's lower (though still high) margins."* Also, sizing the switching opportunity honestly: *"scale-up networking is only about 10% of total system cost."*** [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_08_25_ENC_OpenAI_Jalape%C3%B1o_Better_Than_Nvidia_Blackwell_email.html) · [archived (truncated) PDF copy](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/Semianalysis_on_Jalapeno.html) |

| 08-03 | **Fubon · Sherman Shang / Arthur (hosted by Jefferies), "industry highlights & checks update" — the PRIMARY call transcript behind the "LESS SO FOR BROADCOM" row below** | supply | **bear** | ⚠️ **Machine transcript (ASR) — names garbled; corrected inline on first use.** **The relay told you Broadcom was on the wrong side of this call. The primary tells you WHERE, and it is narrower and more actionable than the relay implied: it is TEST capacity, not foundry, and it is a PUSH-OUT, not a cut.** 🔴 **Verbatim, and this datapoint is not anywhere on this page: "from our supply chain check, we see Broadcom (ASR: 'Borcom') is less bullish in expanding capacity, less bullish in expanding capacity. So Broadcom previously, they plan to add more capacity in the second half. But it seems like the plan is pushed out."** Pressed by an analyst on whether that reflects a Samsung 2nm/advanced-packaging dual-source rather than weak demand, Shang **narrows it himself: "when I said Broadcom is becoming less bullish in expanding capacity, I was talking about THE TESTING CAPACITY. I was talking about the testing capacity. But from the foundry side, I think Broadcom will still use TSMC."** ⚠️ **Two things follow. (a) This is a check-based read on a capacity decision, not a statement about orders — but test capacity is the last thing you add before you ship, so a 2H26 test-capacity push-out is the earliest hard-asset tell the supply chain gives on a 2027 volume expectation. Treat any AVGO-linked test-house guide as at risk (cf. [[NVDA]]'s KYEC guide-down on the same call, from the same mechanism running one generation ahead). (b) It also CUTS AGAINST the page's own 07-28 [[KLAC]]-call datapoint that Broadcom is being qualified at [[SAMSUNG]] Foundry 2nm/3nm — Fubon says "I'm not so sure on that" and keeps Broadcom at TSMC on the foundry side. Both are second-hand; hold them as an open disagreement, not a resolution.** The foundry backdrop he does commit to is a constraint either way: **TSMC 2nm and 3nm supply "will be very, very tight" in 2027**, with TSMC capex rising to **$80-85bn in 2027 from $60-64bn in 2026** and Shang explicitly unsure that is enough — "I'm not sure that is enough to support all the customers demand." 🔴 **On V10, the asymmetry of information is itself the datapoint: "currently, we haven't seen MediaTek's ([[MEDIATEK]]) V10 design. But from our checks, we have already seen Broadcom's V10 design. And it is a gigantic chip with a 8 [2]nm compute dies (ASR: 'a 8 to nanometer compute dies' — read as eight 2nm compute dies, consistent with this page's existing '8 N2 compute dies' mark). And we also heard the TDP of this chip will become 6,000 WATTS, 6,000 watts. And it will also adopt 400 or 448G SerDes (ASR: 'services')."** **The 6,000W TDP is net-new to this page** and is a striking number in its own right — for scale, this page's own AMD Helios read has a whole 72-GPU rack at ~240kW, so a single 6kW accelerator package implies rack- and cooling-level co-design well beyond the current generation. **On SerDes the primary forces a distinction this page must keep clean: Fubon says "Broadcom's 400G is ready" TODAY — which was the explicit basis of his PRIOR view that "in the Google TPU V10, [MediaTek] will probably lose some market share back to Broadcom." That view is now WEAKENED, not by anything Broadcom did, but by MediaTek's earnings-call statement that its own 400G-or-higher SerDes will be ready in 2H27 ("a huge surprise to us"), one year earlier than Fubon assumed — see [MEDIATEK.md](MEDIATEK.md).** ⚠️ **Do NOT net Fubon's "Broadcom's 400G is ready" against this page's standing datapoint that AVGO's **448G** SerDes missed the v9 production deadline (MediaTek delivered 336G). Those are different speed grades and the page should carry both: 400G ready, 448G late.** **Finally, the 2028 expectations bar and the bear question, with Fubon's answer — which declines the bull case as much as the bear: asked about broker 2028 custom-ASIC revenue estimates "ranging somewhere between 35 billion to 52 billion" and whether business could "go back to Broadcom, or indeed Google could change the business model and go like the Alchip or GUC route and do it themselves," Shang's answer is neither: "I think INTEL'S PRODUCTION IS THE BIGGEST RISK… if Intel is unable to enter production, that means MediaTek, they need to go back to TSMC. And I don't think TSMC has the capacity to support Google to do 12 million to 15 million. So when that happens, we will probably see some downside."** ⚠️ **Read that carefully as an AVGO holder: the mechanism by which the $35-52bn 2028 numbers get cut is a TSMC capacity wall, not a reallocation of sockets back to Broadcom. Fubon is not offering AVGO share recapture as the offset — the offset he describes is the whole 2028 TPU number coming down. Vendor-reallocation is explicitly not his swing factor.** Reconfirmed without change: **Tomahawk 6 as one of ASE's three named programs** behind the FoCoS raise to 50k wpm (with [[NVDA]] Vera and [[AMD]] Venice). Google-side TPU volumes/targets on [GOOG.md](GOOG.md); MediaTek share/ASP on [MEDIATEK.md](MEDIATEK.md); Intel EMIB yield/capacity on [INTC.md](INTC.md); TSMC capacity on [TSM.md](TSM.md). (Fubon · Sherman Shang / Arthur (hosted by Jefferies), "industry highlights & checks update", 2026-08-03) [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_08_03_sherman_update_03_08_26.html) |

| 08-02 | **J.P. Morgan · Gokul Hariharan — the GUC/MediaTek upgrades name Broadcom's business model as the thing being dismantled** | competicao | **bear** | ⚠️ **★ THE MOST DIRECT STRUCTURAL ATTACK ON AVGO'S ASIC ECONOMICS PUBLISHED THIS QUARTER, and it is stated as a description of [[GOOG]]'s procurement strategy rather than as a Broadcom call: "we believe that through the evolution from TPUv8 to v10 families, THE BLACK-BOX MODEL FROM US FABLESS VENDOR (WHERE IP, DESIGN SERVICE, MANUFACTURING AND PACKAGING VALUE ADD ARE ALL BUNDLED TOGETHER AT HIGH MARGINS) WILL BE BROKEN DOWN SYSTEMATICALLY IN FAVOR OF A DISAGGREGATED APPROACH"** (JPM, GUC upgrade note, 2026-08-02). **"US fabless vendor" is Broadcom, and "bundled together at high margins" is the AVGO ASIC gross margin. The mechanism JPM describes is Google moving to Customer-Owned Tooling: three engagement models are live under the TPU v10 RFQ — (i) semi-CoT with [[MEDIATEK]] supplying SerDes/D2D/packaging/integration; (ii) compute-die back-end outsourced to pure design-service vendors (GUC, Alchip) on Turnkey-3; (iii) ⚠️ the full break-up, where "ONLY IP BEING SOURCED BY GOOGLE SEPARATELY… WITH FOUNDRY PROCUREMENT BEING HANDLED DIRECTLY BY GOOGLE, SIMILAR TO THE AMAZON TRAINIUM APPROACH."** ⚠️ **Model (iii) is the one that matters for this page: if the customer buys its own wafers from [[TSM]] and its own HBM, the ASIC vendor's revenue line collapses toward IP + services and the manufacturing/packaging margin accrues to the customer. Google ALREADY buys its HBM directly (confirmed on MediaTek's 07-31 call), and JPM notes the full-CoT opportunity "could be EVEN HIGHER" for non-TPU projects "given lower design complexity and IP dependence, AS ALREADY ILLUSTRATED FOR THE GOOGLE AXION CPUs" — i.e. the model has already been executed once at this customer, on CPUs.** **THE COMPETITIVE CLAIM ON v10 SPECIFICALLY: JPM says MediaTek's semi-CoT proposal "appears to offer an ATTRACTIVE TCO ADVANTAGE VERSUS BROADCOM, pointing to a high likelihood of securing the project and even potential share expansion," with the award expected in the coming months and mass production late 2029. Nomura the same day put GUC's chairlady on record as "NOT DENYING the company's opportunity in Google's TPU (which from our industry survey MIGHT BE TPU v.10)."** ⚠️ **How to hold this: it is a sell-side supply-chain read from analysts who cover the Asian challengers and therefore have a directional interest, the RFQ is unresolved, and Broadcom's own share of the current TPU generations is not disputed by any of it. But the argument is no longer "MediaTek is taking share at the margin" — it is "the buyer is re-architecting the contract so that no single vendor can hold that margin again." That is a different and more durable risk, and this page should carry it as the primary bear case on the ASIC franchise's terminal margin, cross-filed to [[GOOG]], [[MEDIATEK]], [[TSM]] and [themes/custom-asic-tpu](themes/custom-asic-tpu.md).** **One counterweight from the same house's own hierarchy: JPM expects "the highest value capture… in the next 3-4 years" to accrue to MediaTek with GUC/Alchip benefiting only "somewhat" — so the disaggregation is not yet a diffusion of the margin to everyone, it is a transfer of it to one named competitor.** Separately, corroborating a standing bear item on this page: **[[ANET]] checks say "supply has NOT improved, AVGO SILICON LEAD TIMES STILL >52 WEEKS"** (UBS, 08-01, already logged) — the franchise is supply-constrained at the same moment its largest ASIC customer is engineering around it. |
