Figure 5 has AVGO at 16.2% (2025) → 22.5% (2026) → 22.3% (2027) of TSMC CoWoS = 109,650 → 263,100 → 421,800 wafers.** The 22.3% supersedes Fubon's own **24-25% / ≈459.6k** mark from 06-05 (moved to Changelog). **Read the two halves separately, because they say different things.** **The volume half is strongly positive and this page has been under-weighting it: on a pool growing 675k → 1,170k → 1,890k wafers, AVGO's TSMC CoWoS wafers roughly 2.4x in 2026 and rise another ~60% in 2027.** In absolute wafers AVGO is the largest gainer after NVDA; the share line flattening near 22% is a denominator effect. **The share half is the negative: the reallocation goes to [[MEDIATEK]] (to 7.3% / 137,700 wafers, from Fubon's own 5.3%) and [[AMD]] (total allocation to 300k, majority via ASE), while [[NVDA]] holds flat at 50.0%.** Fubon states the MediaTek logic explicitly — *"we see that MediaTek is able to get more capacity allocation in 2027, and therefore, we think it is possible that MediaTek could further raise its custom-ASIC market share in 2027"* — and names **MediaTek its top pick for TPU**, calling the recent pullback a re-entry opportunity. That is the sharpest head-to-head statement on the page from a house that covers both. ⚠️ **Two guardrails. (i) This is TSMC-only CoWoS and a broker capacity assumption, not a TSMC or AVGO disclosure. (ii) It is a UNIT/wafer claim and says nothing about revenue share — MS (07-14, 07-26) still models AVGO at ~80% of TPU revenue on higher ASP and gross margin even while conceding MediaTek closes the unit gap. Do not read a wafer-share trim as a revenue-share trim.** **The offsetting positive for AVGO in the same note is on the networking side: ASE's FoCoS capacity is raised to 50kwpm for 2027 "to support CPU and networking customers' demand"** — up from Fubon's own 45k (06-05) and 30k before that, i.e. the offload platform that carries AVGO's switch chipsets and transceivers keeps expanding. Also relevant to the BOM debate this page tracks: Fubon puts **HBM4 at US$31-32/GB for NVDA but US$35-36/GB "for other GPU and custom-ASIC projects," i.e. roughly a doubling of HBM cost in 2027 — and AVGO sits on the wrong side of that spread**, which sharpens the standing HBM-pass-through GM headwind (see Risks / Debate). Fubon's overall stance is **more confident in the CPU, SPE and TPU chains than in the [[NVDA]] GPU chain** on Rubin's 2H26 volume cut. (Fubon "Our advanced packaging, CoWoS forecast adjustments; other updates", 2026-07-28) [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/Our_advanced_packaging_CoWoS_forecast_adjustments_other_up.html) |

AMD Advancing AI 2026") | produto | **bull** | **The most concrete quantification yet of AVGO's scale-up switching socket — and it is sole-source at AMD.** AMD's 72-GPU **Helios** rack runs on **12 Broadcom Tomahawk 6 102.4T switch ASICs across 6 switch trays** (2/tray), single-tier all-to-all, 72 lanes of 200G UALoE per GPU (1.8 TB/s uni-di). AMD has no scale-up switch of its own: "**Because Broadcom is the only merchant provider shipping a 100T switch with 200G SerDes, Broadcom is AMD's only choice for the switch**" — SemiAnalysis frames this as AMD's constraint ("AMD cannot reach extreme codesign"), i.e. a locked socket for AVGO. The mismatch is extra AVGO content too: only **432 of each switch's 512 200G lanes are used** (86.4T of 102.4T) because merchant silicon doesn't divide evenly across 72 GPUs, vs Nvidia's purpose-built **28.8T NVSwitch** which does "with no wastage of bandwidth at all." **Net-new content leg:** AMD's weak 200G SerDes means "**up to 85% of its backplane needs to be retimed, requiring over 550 Broadcom ethernet retimers per rack**" (~85% of scale-up links on Meta's MI455X), sited in the scale-up switch tray — cost/power/assembly pain for AMD, incremental silicon for AVGO (Nvidia's Oberon backplane is fully passive). **MI500 (2027) scales the switch count ~24x:** co-packaged copper + near-packaged optics (9 optical engines × 6.4 Tbit/s = 57.6T scale-up), a flat one-tier **576-GPU / 8-rack** domain built from **288 switches on a 115.2T Tomahawk 6 SKU**. Also relevant to the frontier-lab-concentration thread: AMD is described as "**fighting a war on two fronts**" — merchant GPUs *and* the custom-ASIC complex (TPU/Trainium making external chip sales to large AI labs), i.e. the ASIC franchise AVGO builds is the second front. **Quarter synthesis:** flow turned incrementally more constructive through 06-27/06-29 (Jalapeño2/A16, memory-on-logic, Apollo 20GW, Jefferies BUY reiteration refuting the v9-delay/MediaTek-share bear case, Citi's #2-mega-cap memory-supply rank), then **reversed sharply on 07-01**: JPM (Gokul Hariharan) reports Broadcom's **Pumafish (TPU 9i) program cancelled or postponed**, directly contradicting the 06-29 Jefferies "on-track" framing and ceding 2028-29 v9-generation share to MediaTek (Broadcom retains a v10 role alongside MediaTek's Icefish); JPM/Hariharan also flags a possible v9-family SRAM ASIC ("Merope") going to **Marvell instead of Broadcom**. Both are concrete negative revisions to the custom-silicon share story, layering onto the pre-existing Redburn/HBM-in-BOM and SerDes-timing bear threads. Partially offsetting: **MS (Joe Moore, 06-30)** models **~14GW total 2028 compute** (~9GW TPU per Nowak) — a bull data point on absolute 2028 scale even as *share-mix* risk (Pumafish/Merope) rises — and **MS (Joseph Moore)** prefers AVGO on tightening inventory/supply alongside NVDA. Watch the **July 7 CEO/CFO NDR** (JPM desk) and Jefferies' teased "Revisiting the AVGO Bull Thesis" for management's response to the Pumafish reports. Mainstream press (Barron's 06-01/06-22) still corroborates the longer-run bull case (0.37 PEG, "scalpel vs Swiss Army knife" framing) but the marginal news of the day (07-01) is bearish on custom-silicon share, not demand. **Flow turned constructive again on 07-08:** JPM (Harlan Sur) reiterated Overweight after an investor meeting with Hock Tan / CFO Amie Thuener / IR Ji Yoo — ~50/50 XPU/GPU unit mix at the largest frontier labs next year, customers pulling in XPU tape-outs, Jalapeño validating ASIC execution (~9-mo tape-out) with the next OpenAI XPU taping out "very soon," Tomahawk 6 sold out, and ASIC margins held in-target — a broad rebuttal of the COT/share-loss/margin bear threads; JPM Sur also framed **TPU v9 as on track to ramp CY28** (400G SerDes working), which sits in direct tension with JPM Hariharan's 07-01 "Pumafish cancelled/postponed" report (unresolved intra-house). Separately the Apple–Broadcom 2031 deal was quantified at **$30B+** (Citi/Malik maintain Buy; @markgurman; DIGITIMES 07-09; Bernstein: "doesn't move the needle too much" but positive), with **$1.5B Colorado capex** and **15B+ US-made chips** and a custom-AI-silicon ramp seen ~2029/2030. **The 07-07 primary transcript** (Hock Tan / Amy Tanner, the call underlying Sur's 07-08 note) hardens the bull frame with first-party detail — only ~5 frontier developers = ~80% of demand, XPU units cross GPUs CY27 (~50/50 for the 5 buyers), COT "not a chance"/A8 "still in the Fab," Tomahawk 6 sold out, sole working 400G-copper SerDes, chip-margins-only into the $35B XPV, HBM not consigned. The offsetting bear mark of the week is **GS's 50/50 Broadcom/MediaTek split on the next-gen Google TPU** (07-09) — contested by US investors but another share-cede data point — while **MS (07-10) frames AVGO's AI business growing 80%+ next year and supply-constrained** (OW), a demand-scale positive. **07-11/07-13 add two constructive sector marks on demand (not share):** Mizuho reiterates Outperform (PT $530), with GOOGL's top-of-peer capex revision (+external TPU sales) and META's 14GW-2027E plan (~$200-300B, 40% above cons) both flagged **+ve AVGO**; and MS's *updated* Scale-Up Primer **4x's its own scale-up TAM to ~$70bn by 2030**, staying positive on AVGO into the 2027-28 ASIC ramps + networking pull-through, while endorsing AVGO's conservative copper-extension (200G→400G SerDes, 6m demo) view that pushes broad scale-up CPO to ~2028-29 — i.e. the demand/scale backdrop keeps improving even as the Pumafish/Merope/MediaTek share-mix debate stays open. **07-13/07-14 finally put a bull on the other side of that share debate:** Morgan Stanley (Joseph Moore) published **"In Defense of AVGO's ASIC Share" (reiterate OW, PT $502)** — MediaTek TPU participation is real but displacement fears are premature, AVGO keeps **~80% TPU share** on its HBM/packaging/scale moat (a close #2 to NVDA), and the YTD AVGO/NVDA underperformance is itself the TPU-share overhang unwinding; MS likens it to last year's over-drawn MRVL/Alchip Trainium debate. FinTwit corroborates (@BenBajarin: ">80%", @firstadopter quoting the note), and @dnystedt notes MediaTek's ASIC push is TSMC-wafer-gated — so the one-way "share-loss" narrative is now a two-sided debate. The offsetting bear mark is **@jukan05 (07-15)**: Samsung is weighing back-end design of the I/O die for Google's **2nm TPU v10 "Icefish," which Google is co-designing with MediaTek** (TSMC 1.4nm compute + Samsung 2nm I/O, MP ~2028) — so MediaTek's *next-gen* (v10) role keeps progressing even as MS defends near-term v7/v8 share. On networking, MS's scale-up-TAM raise gets independent corroboration (@QQ_Timmy: scale-up market >$70bn by 2030, 4x the ~$17bn-2029 est.) and AVGO's CPO/optical-engine positioning is reaffirmed by an ex-Intel expert (@AlphaSenseInc: AVGO + Intel strongest at CPO/NPO optical engines; NPO temporary, CPO the long-term path). **07-15→07-20:** MS (Joe Moore, "US memory selloff = entry point," 07-20) names AVGO with NVDA **"the best value in the market"** (compute over memory), valuing AVGO at **28x CY27e MW EPS $17.92 / ~26x non-GAAP $19.59** (a valuation frame, no PT change — MS OW $502 stands). Two read-throughs: Octahedron's 2Q26 LP call (07-15) cites "Avago" as the spectacular-business comp memory should re-rate toward; and an ex-Intel VP expert call "EMIB vs CoWoS" (07-16) flags Google "diversifying beyond Broadcom" on TPU package design toward **Intel EMIB-T as a 2nd source** — a marginal packaging-diversification nuance on the standing Google-share-cede debate. **07-21/07-24 (SemiAnalysis, two notes — the networking leg of the thesis gets its hardest numbers yet):** the AMD Advancing-AI teardown (07-24) makes AVGO the **sole-source scale-up switch supplier to AMD's Helios rack** — "Broadcom is AMD's only choice" as the only merchant 100T switch with 200G SerDes — at **12 Tomahawk 6 102.4T ASICs across 6 trays per 72-GPU rack**, plus a **net-new retimer leg of >550 Broadcom Ethernet retimers/rack** because AMD's SerDes forces up to 85% of the backplane to be retimed, scaling to **288 switches on a 115.2T TH6 SKU across 576 GPUs/8 racks on MI500 (2027)**. Read together with the 06-25 BTG "no strong competition in switching" call and Hock's 07-07 "Tomahawk 6 sold out, no competitor at TH6," the merchant-networking leg is now the least-contested part of the AVGO story even as the Google-ASIC-share debate stays open — and note the socket sits at *AMD*, i.e. AVGO monetises merchant-GPU share gains it doesn't design. The Meta Infra note (07-21) cuts both ways and both legs are now on the page: negative on Rivos/COT bypass ambition (already logged, heavily hedged), but positive on sockets — Grand Teton "**increased their reliance on Broadcom**" (four PCIe switches/tray), and the DSF→NSF fabric shift moved Meta's spend **within** AVGO's portfolio (Jericho3-AI/Ramon3 → Tomahawk 5) rather than away from it, at the cost of admitting whiteboxes (Celestica) alongside Arista. Two backfill/structural markers added in the same pass, explicitly *not* current reads: **AWS's Sep-2025 decision not to use Broadcom for Trainium was made on price, not capability** ("Broadcom… has mopped up almost all of the major AI ASIC sockets"; Marvell/Alchip chosen as "somewhat weaker technology partners" offering "much more attractive pricing"), and **Google — unlike Amazon — runs its HBM through Broadcom's balance sheet**, the dated origin of the HBM-in-BOM share-cede mechanism Redburn identified in June (SemiAnalysis 2025-09-03); plus **AVGO's ASIC group alone spends an estimated $200-500M/yr on EDA tools/IP/emulation**, among the largest single EDA customers in the world (SemiAnalysis EDA primer, 2026-05-21) — a scale proxy for the design franchise. **08-03 (run-inbox, the Fubon PRIMARY call transcript) closes the window with the first hard-asset negative on AVGO itself, and with the bull case for a share recapture withdrawn by the same analyst who used to make it. The negative: Broadcom is "less bullish in expanding capacity" — specifically TEST capacity, with the planned 2H26 add "pushed out." That is narrower than the relay's blanket "less so for Broadcom" but harder, because test capacity is the last thing added before volume ships. The withdrawn bull case: Fubon's prior view that AVGO takes Google V10 share back from MediaTek rested only on Broadcom having 400G SerDes ready while MediaTek did not; MediaTek's 2H27 readiness (announced on its own call) removes the premise, and Fubon does not replace it. And when handed the bear question directly — could Google shift back to Broadcom, or in-house via Alchip/GUC, given 2028 broker ASIC estimates spanning $35-52bn — his answer names neither: the risk is Intel's EMIB production, because failure there sends MediaTek back to a TSMC that cannot supply 12-15mn TPUs, and the 2028 numbers come down for everyone. ⚠️ So the honest read of this call for AVGO is that its accelerator franchise is being priced off someone else's execution (Intel's, MediaTek's) rather than its own, while the parts of the ticker that clearly hold up are the switch/networking franchise (Tomahawk 6 is one of only three programs funding ASE's 50k-wpm expansion) and the V10 design itself, which Fubon has seen and MediaTek's he has not. One open disagreement left deliberately unresolved: Samsung 2nm dual-sourcing (07-28 KLAC call, yes) vs Fubon (still TSMC). **08-16/08-17 close the window on the two threads that have run through it all quarter, and neither moves a number.** On the ASIC franchise, **Mizuho reiterates Outperform $530 and names four programs ramping 2027E+ (TPU/MTIA/Jalepeno/Baltra)** while making AVGO one of only two named drivers of a **>75% y/y CoWoS 2027E** — supportive, additive, nothing superseded. On the financing thread the development is more interesting: **Epoch AI re-derived the AI XPV structure from public disclosure and landed on this page's exact numbers** ($6bn/$24bn/$4.5bn at T+1pp/5.75%/8.5%; $29bn max exposure), which **converts the $29bn from JPM's read of the 10-Q into a corroborated figure** and adds the three pieces the page lacked — **a draw schedule (~16 stages, ~$24bn out by summer 2027, so exposure peaks in mid-2027 rather than now)**, **a default waterfall that makes AVGO SECOND-loss behind used-rack residual value**, and **a 2.75pp upper bound on what the backstop is worth to lenders**. Net: the bull case (vendor-supported financing is what makes the TPU deployment fundable, and demand durability is not financing-constrained) and the bear case (a $29bn contingent exposure whose recovery assumption is un-modelled and whose peak is still ahead) are now built on the SAME disclosed structure rather than on competing guesses — which is the most useful thing that happened to this debate all quarter. **🔴 08-14/08-20 add the market infrastructure underneath that structure and one dated flag, and both belong in the synthesis rather than only in Risks.** **"Chip financing" via 4(a)(2) PRIVATE PLACEMENTS went from ZERO to $35BN year-on-year** (MS US Credit Strategy, 08-14) — inside an issuance table where US HY went 15→41 (+176%), leveraged loans 2→10 (+394%), non-USD IG hyperscalers 15→62 (+313%) and the total 189→382 (+102%). ➜ **That is what makes vendor-supported structures like the $30bn Anthropic/TPU backstop repeatable rather than exceptional: constructive for ASIC volume, and simultaneously a measure of how much contingent, off-public-market exposure the complex is taking on. MS's own thesis is that financing shifts "increasingly toward chips and components, with a larger role for private capital and high-quality credit support," with wider spreads "the natural release valve" and a stated preference for collateral-backed risk.** ✅ **The offsetting fact, and it should never be quoted apart from the $35bn: the [[NVDA]]+AVGO credit complex widened only ~20bp YTD versus +26/+29bp for the hyperscaler buckets and +1bp for broad IG — the market is differentiating inside tech and charging the chip VENDORS less than the buyers of their chips.** **🗓️ And MS's tracker names the print: *"For Single Name, ORCL and AVGO remain front and center ahead of their respective September [reporting dates]"* (08-20), into *"a busy fall and continued spread widening"* — so 09-02 is now a credit event as well as an equity one, which matters because the backstop draws in ~16 stages with ~$24bn expected paid out by summer 2027. Semiconductor issuance itself ran $42bn YTD (+57%).** **🔴 Separately, the competitive debate moved forward two generations: BofA's Asia call (08-20) reports a LIVE contest over whether [[AMD]], Broadcom, Alchip or GUC penetrates the [[GOOG]] TPU chain at V10 (~two years out), against MediaTek's claim to have been on V10's "triplet" architecture with Google since 2H last year and to own the integration.** ⚠️ **Two-sided and neither side is settled: Broadcom is still a named candidate two generations past the v9 loss (incumbency not conceded), but it is one of four contenders where it used to be the default and the dated head start is MediaTek's. The same call is the most directly bullish demand statement of the month for an ASIC franchise — hyperscaler capex still trending up with "no slowdown" despite the GOOG/META headlines, and spend rotating toward ASICs and toward monetisable inference because memory cost is rising while capex can no longer be funded from free cash flow. Held as a debate and a mechanism, not as a design win or a number: no AVGO rating, PT or estimate attaches to any of it.** ⚠️ **Guard-rail: MS's ~$1.4trn 2027 capex figure is an AGGREGATE across AMZN/GOOG/META/MSFT/ORCL/SPCX and NO per-name split is taken from that exhibit (column-interleaved chart) — it must not be fed into the FY27 AI-revenue debate as a customer-level number.**

## Changelog
- **2026-08-31 (/wiki-ingest, pass 3) — UBS PRICE TARGET DRIFT: $485 → $470, AND THE REASON IS ON THE RECORD.** This page carried **UBS · Timothy Arcuri at Buy / PT $485** (last restated 07-27 in the advanced-packaging deep dive, *"Buy/PT $485 unchanged"*). **Superseded 2026-08-31: Buy maintained, PT cut to $470**, solely because UBS trimmed ~$5bn from its F2027 AI revenue estimate (to ~$130bn) after checks showed it had been *"too aggressive on AVGO's TPU unit shipments"* — now 6.4mn CY27 units. **The $485 is retired here; the rating is unchanged and no other UBS estimate on this page was overwritten.**
- **2026-08-31 (/wiki-ingest, pass 3) — THE JALAPEÑO TAPE-OUT CONFLICT IS RESOLVED AND THE EARLIER FLAG IS RETIRED.** Pass 2 logged guard (e): *"SemiAnalysis's 'taped out in nine months' on Jalapeño conflicts with the AVGO page's dated ~16-month design-to-tape-out sequence — flagged, not reconciled; the dated sequence wins."* **Barclays (Tom O'Malley, 08-31) reports from Hot Chips that the nine months is measured FROM INITIAL RTL, not from design start. Different measurement windows, no conflict: both figures stand and neither "wins". Guard (e) is withdrawn.** The live question moves on, in Barclays' own words: *"It is unclear how much AVGO helped with the core design, but we believe this is the key debate from here."*
- **2026-08-31 (/wiki-ingest) — JPM PRICE TARGET: THE $500 IS RETIRED, THE $580 IS ADOPTED.** The 08-31 JPM research alert arrived internally inconsistent — structured field *"Price Target (Dec-26): $580.00"* against body text *"Reiterate OW and $500 PT"* — and this page's first pass recorded both and adopted neither. **Resolved the same day by a second, independent JPM channel: sector specialist Joshua Meyers quoting Harlan Sur's own basis, *"a $580 TP on 23x $25.35 earnings power exiting F27."* 23 × $25.35 = $583, so the arithmetic closes on $580.** ➜ **The page now carries JPM at $580 (Dec-26) with the basis attached. THE SUPERSEDED VALUE, PRESERVED HERE: "$500", from the body text of the 2026-08-31 alert — treated as an error in that alert, not as a prior target.** ⚠️ **Both marks remain sales/alert channels rather than the research PDF; if the primary ever shows $500 the resolution above must be reopened.**
- **2026-08-31 (/wiki-ingest) — the FY27 AI-revenue "bar" is now recorded as a RANGE, not a number.** This page previously carried the bar loosely as *"materially above >$100bn."* It is now logged explicitly as **four different house bars that do not agree — $120bn (JPM buyside survey landing zone) / ~$120bn (MS house estimate) / $130-140bn (Jefferies' read of expectations) / $130bn+ (JPM house model)**, with *">$150bn"* as the tail expectation MS names and declines to underwrite. **Superseded framing: a single implied bar.** The spread is retained deliberately — it is the mechanism behind a beat-and-raise trading down.
- **2026-08-25 — 6 SOURCES (1 SemiAnalysis primary on Jalapeño, 1 MS Asia AI-supply-chain note, 1 Mizuho de-spec note, 3 MS TMT **CREDIT** notes of which two are archive backfill). 8 intra-quarter rows, 2 Signal-vs-management rows extended, 1 Risks sub-bullet, 6 Sources links. NOTHING SUPERSEDED: no AVGO rating, PT, guide, reported number or share mark was overwritten.**
  🔴 **THE HEADLINE ITEM IS A VALIDATION, NOT A THREAT. SemiAnalysis was let into OpenAI's lab and benchmarked Jalapeño — the ASIC AVGO builds — on A0 silicon nine months after a November-2025 CoWoS tape-out, and it beat every NVIDIA, AMD and Google part they have tested on tokens per MW. For THIS page the load-bearing detail is not the benchmark, it is the BILL OF MATERIALS: the scale-up fabric is six 102.4T Tomahawk 6 ASICs per rack for the local domain plus two global switch trays, 128 ASICs per rack, a 2,048-XPU scale-up domain over copper + 1.6T optics + OCS. AVGO captures the compute die AND the switching. Production ramps through 2027, back-end loaded to Q4-2027; the next milestone is 100MW.** ⚠️ **The same article prices the argument honestly and both halves are recorded: on perf/TCO Jalapeño and Vera Rubin are head-to-head, and *"part of this TCO advantage comes from trading Nvidia's high margins for Broadcom's lower (though still high) margins"* — i.e. the ASIC wins partly by being cheaper, which is exactly the structural GM-dilution risk already on this page, restated by a bull.**
  🔴 **THE CREDIT BLOCK IS THE BIGGEST ANALYTICAL ADDITION, AND IT COMES FROM A DESK THIS PAGE HAD NOT YET NAMED. Morgan Stanley TMT CREDIT (Lindsay A Tyler) is the specialist that actually sized the contingent exposure: $19.3–24.2bn of maximum residual-value support PER GW ⇒ ~$290–480bn across the >20GW AI XPV platform (~$350bn headline on 06-19; "could surpass ~$300bn" on 08-05 with a second tranche reported). Treated as debt that is 3.3x gross adjusted leverage by FY28E against Moody's 2.5x threshold — though MS's own conclusion is that "the issue therefore would more be a hypothetical capital call, rather than the ratings impact."** ⚠️ **AND THE ARC IS THE POINT: the SAME desk recommended AVGO bonds on 05-29 ("a potential entry point"), closed every one of those recommendations on 06-19 once the platform was sized at >20GW, and by 08-05 called the level "not … a compelling re-entry point." Three weeks turned a buy into a close. That is a cleaner read on how the market re-priced the AI XPV disclosure than any equity note currently on this page.**
  🔴 **THE COLLATERAL SENTENCE, RECORDED VERBATIM BECAUSE IT IS THE UNMODELLED INPUT: the tail risk is "heightened by the lack of an established TPU/XPU secondary market. TPUs appear less fungible and liquid than GPUs, with third-party sales only recently disclosed by [[GOOG]]." AVGO's backstop is second-loss against a residual value in a market that does not yet exist.**
  ⚠️ **BROKER MIS-DETECTION CORRECTED AT INGEST: the file titled "SemiAnalysis: Memory De-Spec, WFE, 800V Uncertainty with Copper/Optics Upside" (2026-08-24) is a MIZUHO note by Vijay Rakesh reporting a dinner with SemiAnalysis president Doug O'Laughlin — it is NOT a SemiAnalysis publication, and it is attributed to Mizuho everywhere on this page. Its AVGO content: Outperform, PT $530 unchanged (px $358.76), GM/OM ~77%/67%, FCF toward ~$52bn by F26E, AI revenue to ~$157bn by F28E, custom-silicon share ~70-80% of a $150-170bn 2028E market — plus the de-spec read-across that "the lower DRAM/GPU content could allow ~20-30% more GPU/ASIC shipments" and better GPU/ASIC margins, with [[GOOG]] confirmed by Mizuho's checks to have "scaled down TPUv8 HBM3e to 8Hi."**
  ⚠️ **CONTRADICTION LOGGED, NOT RESOLVED (inside the MS Asia note itself): Exhibit 2 labels TPU v10 "Sunfish" while Exhibit 3 labels v8i as Sunfish and v10 as Icefish. Per the standing rule that a broker's front matter goes one revision stale against its exhibits — and because Exhibit 3 is the one consistent with JPM (08-20) and @sssjeffpu (08-24) already on this page — the Exhibit-3 naming is used and the discrepancy is left open.**

  🔴 **THE COMPETITIVE ITEM — THE GOOGLE DEBATE HAS MOVED FORWARD TWO GENERATIONS, AND IT IS REPORTED AS LIVE RATHER THAN SETTLED. BofA reports an active investor debate over whether [[AMD]], BROADCOM, ALCHIP or GUC penetrates the [[GOOG]] TPU supply chain ABOUT TWO YEARS OUT, i.e. at TPU V10; the counter-evidence against a new entrant is MediaTek's own feedback that it has been on V10's "TRIPLET" architecture with Google SINCE 2H LAST YEAR and DOES THE INTEGRATION.** ⚠️ **Both readings written onto the page, because it genuinely is two-sided: CONSTRUCTIVE — Broadcom is still a named candidate at V10, two generations past the v9 order loss this page has been trading, so the incumbency is not conceded (the forward extension of MS/Moore's "In Defense of AVGO's ~80% ASIC share"). UNCOMFORTABLE — AVGO is one of FOUR contenders at a customer where it was the default, and the party with the dated architectural head start is neither Broadcom nor the challengers but MediaTek.** ⚠️ **"V10" was deliberately NOT hardened into a design-win or a share number: BofA frames it as a debate about what happens ~two years out, from a broker-hosted call with no rating attached, and the MediaTek/[[GOOG]] specifics belong on those pages (not edited here). Also logged: investors like MediaTek, GUC and Alchip in design services, with ALCHIP LEAST PREFERRED on communication track record; BofA prefers AI over non-AI (vs UMC).**
  🔴 **THE MOST DIRECTLY BULLISH DEMAND STATEMENT OF THE MONTH FOR AN ASIC FRANCHISE, from the same call, and it arrives in the same breath as the V10 contest: hyperscaler capex is still trending UP ("not seeing any of the slowdown from hyperscaler investment despite… news talking about [[GOOG]] as well as [[META]] might slowing down their investment or cutting their head count in the frontier model development"), GPU/CPU demand is "trending better as compared to people's expectation ahead of the earnings season back in early July," and into 2H26/2027 spend rotates toward ASICs because *"the component cost especially in memory is just getting higher, while they will not be able to fully fulfil their own capex with their free cash flow… they will need to either raise more capital externally, WHICH WILL FORCE THEM TO BE THINKING ABOUT THE MONETIZATION OF AI MODELS instead of continuing to make a huge investment on GPUs for the frontier model training."*** ➜ **Memory-cost inflation plus a funding constraint is the mechanism for merchant-GPU-to-custom-ASIC substitution and for inference over frontier training. Logged as a mechanism, not as a number.**
  🔴 **THE CREDIT ITEM, AND IT IS THE MARKET INFRASTRUCTURE UNDERNEATH THIS PAGE'S CONTINGENT-EXPOSURE RISK RATHER THAN GENERIC MACRO: "chip financing" via 4(a)(2) PRIVATE PLACEMENTS WENT FROM ZERO TO $35BN YEAR-ON-YEAR (MS US Credit Strategy, 08-14), inside a table where US HY went 15→41 (+176%), US leveraged loans 2→10 (+394%), non-USD IG hyperscalers 15→62 (+313%), EUR HY 0→2 and the total 189→382 (+102%).** ➜ **Filed two-sided, and both sides are on the page: a $35bn-and-growing private-placement channel dedicated to chip financing is what makes vendor-supported structures like the $30bn Anthropic/TPU backstop REPEATABLE rather than exceptional (constructive for ASIC volume), and it is simultaneously a measure of how much contingent, off-public-market exposure the complex is accumulating (the risk the bullet exists to track). MS's own thesis — financing shifting "increasingly toward chips and components, with a larger role for private capital and high-quality credit support," capacity ample but increasingly price-sensitive, "wider spreads the natural release valve," a stated preference for collateral-backed risk — points the same way.**
  ✅ **THE OFFSET, RECORDED SO THE $35BN IS NEVER QUOTED ALONE: YTD spread change (bp) by bucket — HQ Hyperscalers +29 · Hyperscalers incl. [[ORCL]] and [[SPCX]] +26 · [[NVDA]]+BROADCOM +20 · Semis +2 · Tech +11 · IG +1. The NVDA/AVGO credit complex widened 20bp YTD, LESS than either hyperscaler bucket — the credit market is differentiating inside tech and charging the chip VENDORS less than the buyers of their chips. Semiconductor issuance itself ran $42bn YTD, +57% (monthly 5/0/0/8/0/25/0/5 Jan→Aug).**

[[MEDIATEK]] rises to 7.3% / 137,700 wafers (from Fubon's own 5.3%) and [[AMD]]'s total allocation rises to 300k (majority via ASE, not TSMC), while [[NVDA]] holds flat at 50.0%. Fubon states the mechanism explicitly — "we see that MediaTek is able to get more capacity allocation in 2027, and therefore, we think it is possible that MediaTek could further raise its custom-ASIC market share in 2027" — and names MediaTek its TOP PICK FOR TPU, calling the recent pullback a re-entry opportunity. That is the sharpest available statement of the head-to-head from a house covering both sides.** **⚠️ Two guardrails recorded so the cut is not over-read: (i) this is TSMC-ONLY CoWoS and a broker capacity-model assumption, not a TSMC or AVGO disclosure — Fubon's "total allocation" figures elsewhere in the same note (NVDA 1.03mn, AMD 300k) include SPIL/ASE and are a different basis; (ii) it is a UNIT/wafer claim and carries no revenue-share implication. MS (Joe Moore 07-14, Charlie Chan 07-26) still models AVGO at ~80% of TPU REVENUE on higher ASP and gross margin while conceding MediaTek closes the unit gap — the two views are compatible and both remain on the page.** **Offsetting positive in the same note: ASE's FoCoS capacity raised to 50kwpm for 2027 "to support CPU and networking customers' demand," superseding Fubon's own 45k (06-05, itself raised from 30k) — that is the platform carrying AVGO's switch chipsets and transceivers, so the networking leg gets more capacity even as the accelerator leg gets a share trim.** **One BOM datapoint that sharpens an existing bear: Fubon puts HBM4 at US$31-32/GB for NVDA but US$35-36/GB "for other GPU and custom-ASIC projects," i.e. HBM cost roughly doubling in 2027 — AVGO sits on the expensive side of that ~US$4/GB spread, which is a quantified worsening of the HBM-pass-through GM headwind already logged in Risks and in the Anthropic ASP work (~$2.5k of HBM in a ~$12k TPU ASP).** **Second source, one line only and deliberately not inflated: MS TMT webcast (07-28) — Joe Moore's rank order across his whole coverage is NVDA > AVGO > MU > SNDK, "nothing else that's wildly compelling," on the argument the four are "so much cheaper than every other name that has some speculative potential." No rating, PT or estimate change; consistent with his own 07-14 "In Defense of AVGO's ASIC Share."** Cross-filed [[TSM]], [[NVDA]], [[MEDIATEK]], [[AMD]], [themes/cowos-packaging](themes/cowos-packaging.md), [themes/custom-asic-tpu](themes/custom-asic-tpu.md). |
